### MOST BEAUTIFUL DOCTRINE

<margin>Demons seek to instill ingratitude.</margin>
<margin>Demons leave signs of their presence.</margin>
...at least [the person] should not be the cause of lust <note>The text suggests that the victim's own moral state or "libidinis" (desire/lust) could provide a foothold for the demon's return.</note>, otherwise the liberator will be forced to labor all over again. They [the demons] are also accustomed, after they have departed, to persuade the liberated person as much as they can that they were never actually possessed, specifically so that the person might be ungrateful to God, allowing the spirits to re-enter. They also try to persuade those they have left that they haven't actually departed, for the same reason as before; they do this through certain lingering effects which they leave behind or cause within the body. But against this, the **Priest** about to read the Exorcisms should command them by the power of God not to pretend to be occupiers if they are not; and he should advise the liberated person as much as possible to believe they are free and to give thanks to God.

<margin>Diligence to be applied in the conjuration.</margin>
In the conjuration, however, when he feels the serpent <note>original: *serpentē*; a common metaphorical term for the demonic presence or movement felt physically within the possessed person's body.</note> trembling under his consecrated hand, let him command the possessed person to make known whatever the spirit says to them. And when the spirit begins to speak within the possessed, let him occasionally begin to adjure <term>adjure: to charge or command someone earnestly or solemnly, often under oath or the threat of a curse</term> it, questioning it about the aforementioned matters as he sees fit, asking for its name and its companions—especially when it begins to speak through the mouth of the possessed, even sometimes interrupting the reading of the prayers.

<margin>Investigation to be made in the interrogations.</margin>
For immediately as any spirit manifests itself, he ought to inquire by adjuration who it is, who its companions are, the cause of its entry, the time of entry, who is destined to expel it, and who are its enemies in heaven and in the abyss. He should write down its name and the names of its companions, as he is able to get them, on slips of paper, so that if they are stubborn in obeying, he may burn those papers with blessed fire.

<margin>It is most grievous to demons for their names to be burned.</margin>
For these things are most grievous to a demon, because they bring him disgrace and serve as a reminder of Gehenna <note>original: *gehennæ*; a biblical term referring to the place of eternal punishment or Hell.</note>. When the demon has confessed these things, at the priest's discretion, let him make the demon ratify what has been confessed.

<margin>Let the **Exorcist** not be a trivial or curious investigator in interrogations.</margin>
However, the **Priest** should take care not to be a trivial, curious, or superfluous interrogator, lest God withdraw the demon's obedience from him. After he has obtained every desired confession from the devil, let him always adjure it to depart, asking after every individual conjuration whether it wishes to leave. When the demon professes that it is willing to leave, the **Priest** must take care not to give it leave to depart unless a command is first made that it leave none of its companions behind in the possessed person, and that it does not return to them hereafter; and he should demand a promise from the spirit regarding this. Yet not even with this, or when the **Priest** has commanded something else...

It also warns the priest against asking irrelevant questions and highlights the demon's strategy of feigning presence or causing ingratitude to regain entry.</summary>

### *Concerning certain other deceptions which demons*
### *use to deceive the Exorcist, which must*
### *necessarily be known by all Exorcists.*
### *Chapter XIII.*

<margin>
The malice
and worthlessness
of certain
demons.

The notable
cunning of
demons.

Exorcist beware
here, lest he
be deceived.

Demons
tremble at
the first

Exorcist.

There are certain demons who have such a great hatred for holy words that they depart from the possessed bodies before they are even conjured. Some, however, wait for the conjuration, which they are afterwards unable to withstand. Yet some are so malicious that they cause the Exorcist to be worn out for a long period of time in vain, so that it appears to the sick person that they are not actually possessed <note>The demon "plays dead" or hides its presence so the victim and the priest believe the illness is purely natural rather than supernatural.</note>; they do this so that the Exorcist, out of labor and exhaustion, will cease the exorcism and depart from there. If he does this, they consider themselves to have obtained the victory—especially when he gives up the exorcism because of weariness. Against this, the Exorcist must be steadfast and not fail in faith; he must be diligent in the conjuration and must not cease until he sees the signs of liberation. Some demons, after they are detected, hide themselves and leave the body free from all infestation, so that the sick person thinks himself entirely free. Against this, the Exorcist must be cautious, persevering in the conjuration for three, four, or six continuous hours, and more, until he has obtained the victory. For even if the demons seem to fear more in the first instance than in the second, they are nevertheless weakened daily; therefore, if the Energumen <term>Energumen: a person believed to be possessed by an evil spirit</term> is not liberated the first time, let him follow through a second and third time until the person is liberated entirely. Some demons place every obstacle they can in the way of the sick person, so that they cannot have themselves exorcised...

<header>MOST BEAUTIFUL TEACHING</header>

...accomplish. Some, however, try to persuade others that these are merely natural illnesses, and that this whole matter is a deceitful invention <note>original: *inventio trufatica*; "trufatica" refers to trickery, nonsense, or a mocking delusion.</note>.
<margin>Demons make the possessed sleep, and why.</margin>
Others make the sick person fall asleep in the middle of the conjuration by showing them some vision—for instance, of the Crucified Christ or other Saints. They do this to distract the sick person’s mind from the holy readings and to withdraw themselves from the power of the conjuration; by making the person appear peacefully free of the spirit, they intend to remain hidden within that body. Against this, the Exorcist should strive to disturb them with Exorcisms, commands, and harsh threats, so that they cannot remain hidden. 
<margin>Another trick of demons against the Exorcist.</margin>
Certain demons reveal the witchcraft <term>maleficium: harmful magic or a curse believed to be caused by a demon or sorcerer</term> done to the sick person, uncovering the perpetrators and even the method by which it can be undone. They do this so that the Exorcist might attempt to break the curse in an unlawful or superstitious manner. But the Exorcist must be cautious in this, as in all other things, ensuring he does not attempt to solve the problem by using another form of witchcraft. 
<margin>Why demons do not fear the reception of the Eucharist.</margin>
Nor should anyone wonder if a demon is sometimes not troubled during the reception of the Eucharist <term>Eucharist: the sacrament of Holy Communion</term>. For after the demon has entered, it gradually begins to look down upon and despise holy things; it does not fear them. Indeed, it even speaks of the mysteries of our faith and confesses the divine majesty; it takes over the person being tormented and speaks in their heart, making it seem as if the person is barely disturbed by the sacrament at all. And some demons, after long tormenting a person, if they are not expelled, speak almost constantly through that person's mouth and cease harming them physically, only to be finally caught later on. These points are taken almost literally from the *Sacerdotale* <note>original: *Sacerdotali*; likely referring to a contemporary priest's manual or liturgical handbook.</note>.

---

### Concerning those things which must be known by the Exorcist, so that he can cure those who are bewitched and possessed.
### Chapter XIV.

<margin>Things to be known by Exorcists.</margin>

He must also know what signs appear when he adjures the victim, the manner the devil takes when exiting possessed bodies, and finally, he must...

...to recognize the malice and deceptions that demons use to trick the Exorcist; regarding all of which, beyond what was said above, we have spoken in our *Club* <note>original: *Fuste*; referring to the author’s manual titled *Fustis Daemonum* (The Club of Demons), a famous 16th-century exorcism handbook.</note> in chapters 11 and 12.

### *Regarding the place and time in which the possessed are to be exorcised, Chapter XV.*

<margin>The Exorcist should not perform exorcisms in private homes.</margin>
<margin>The Church is the place dedicated to conjuration.</margin>
<margin>The time for adjuring demons.</margin>
<margin>Demons greatly fear the prayers of a good priest.</margin>
An Exorcist must take care, as much as possible, not to perform this duty of adjuring demons in private homes without grave necessity, lest it give occasion for scandal to the simple <note>original: *pusillis*; literally "the little ones," a biblical term for the spiritually vulnerable or common folk</note>; but rather to exorcise in a church, or in another place dedicated to God or to some Saint. This is advised partly because in private homes, in most cases, women are present, whose company should be avoided by Exorcists with the utmost care, lest they fall into the snare of the devil; partly because many shameful things happen there that are better left unspoken than brought up here; and also because the church is the proper place designated where the possessed <term>Energumeni: from the Greek "energoumenos," referring to those "worked upon" or troubled by an evil spirit</term> ought to be exorcised and demons adjured to withdraw from the obsessed bodies. In the church, they fear and tremble more at the holy words of God than elsewhere, since it is the house of prayer in which God dwells, and where our prayers and supplications are heard better than in any other place. Regarding the time, I say that no fixed time is set; therefore, when charity compels you, you may exorcise at any time. However, it is beneficial for Exorcists to perform the rite early in the morning after Mass; and the holier the days are—such as the feasts of the Birth of Christ, His Resurrection and Ascension, the feast of Pentecost, and the like; the feasts of the Blessed Virgin Mary, the Apostles, and other solemnities or vigils—the more effective the rites will be. Take care, however, not to begin exorcising without some preparation, since demons fear prayers made by a devout Exorcist much more than those by a lax <note>original: *dissoluto*; referring to an exorcist who lacks spiritual discipline or is "loose" in his habits</note> one, as we said above in Chapter 1.

<summary>This page outlines the protocol for where and when exorcisms should be performed, emphasizing the sanctity of the church over private homes and the effectiveness of holy feast days.</summary>

<page-num>320</page-num>
<header>MOST EXCELLENT TEACHING <note>original: *DOCTR. PULCHERR.*; an abbreviation for *Doctrina Pulcherrima*</note></header>

### It is demonstrated that a person can be troubled by unclean spirits: where it is shown what a "seized" person <term>Arreptitius: from the Latin *arripere* (to snatch or seize); a term used for someone physically overtaken by a demon</term> is. Chapter XVI.

<margin>The foolish wonder of some.</margin>
It should be understood, for the sake of those who are accustomed to wondering when they see certain righteous people physically troubled by unclean spirits, that they do not understand for what reasons the Blessed God allows this to happen. Nor do they consider those prerequisites by which this is made possible, which are: the permissive will of God; the knowledge and power of demons; and the freedom of the will, by which demons are able to perform wonders. 

<margin>Demons can inhabit human bodies.</margin>
Indeed, demons can inhabit a person as far as the body is concerned (as daily experience testifies), but this pertains more to the nature of a punishment <note>original: *pœnæ*</note> than to a fault <note>original: *culpæ*</note>, according to Saint Thomas <note>referring to St. Thomas Aquinas, the influential 13th-century theologian</note>. Furthermore, the physical punishments of this life do not always follow from a fault; rather, they are sometimes inflicted upon sinners, and sometimes upon those who have not sinned, according to the depth of God’s judgments. This is clear from the example of the man born blind in John 9 <note>In the Gospel of John, Jesus explains that the man's blindness was not due to his or his parents' sin, but so the works of God might be displayed</note>. Therefore, those who are thus obsessed by demons are usually called "the seized" <note>original: *Arreptitii*</note>.

<margin>What it means to be "seized."</margin>
As to what it means for a person to be "seized," I say that it is nothing other than for a person (by God’s permission) to be so possessed by a demon that the demon uses the person's limbs according to its own will, both internally and externally. This can indeed happen in five ways, as we have stated in our *Compendium* <note>Referring to the author's previous work, likely the *Compendium Maleficarum*</note>, Book 2, chapter 11. Historically, it is recorded that this has happened for five general reasons, though it can occur for countless other specific reasons of which we have spoken in that same place. Therefore, we 

<margin>Why demons do not speak Latin.</margin>
stop here, referring the reader to the aforementioned chapter. There, the reader will also see why demons in possessed bodies in these times of ours do not speak Hebrew, Greek, and Latin, and many other things worth knowing.

<header>AGAINST EVIL SPIRITS <note>original: *IN MALIGNOS SPIR.*; an abbreviation of *In Malignos Spiritus*</note> 321</header>

### That some possessed persons are found who are never freed, and concerning the reasons why this happens. Chapter XVII.

<margin>Certain possessed persons are never freed.</margin>
It should be noted that many people possessed by demons are found who are never freed from those same unclean spirits, by God's permission. Seeing this, some people are filled with wonder, being ignorant of the reason for this fact. Therefore, for a greater explanation, it should be known that although this can happen due to six general causes, there is yet a seventh, above which our judgment is suspended <note>This suggests a reason known only to God's secret wisdom</note>. For, if someone is not freed, it is either:
<margin>Smallness of faith hinders the liberation of the possessed.</margin>
because of the smallness of faith of the bystanders, or of those presenting the sick person; or because of the sins of those suffering the vexation <term>vexationem: a technical term for the physical and mental harassment by a demon</term>; or because of the negligence of those who fail to apply suitable remedies; or because of some flaw in the faith of the Exorcist; or because of the reverence of virtues existing in another; or for the sake of the purification or merit of those suffering the vexation. The first cause, therefore, is the meager faith of the bystanders or those presenting the victim, of which the father of that lunatic spoke in Matthew 17 and Mark 9: *I believe, Lord; help my unbelief.* <note>original: *Credo domine, adjuva incredulitatem meam*</note> And to the surrounding crowd, Jesus Christ said there: *O faithless and perverse generation, how long shall I be with you?* The second cause is the sins of the person suffering the vexation; regarding this, Christ rebuked that son in the same passage. Here Saint Jerome says that he
<margin>One's own sins hinder the liberation of the possessed.</margin>
had been oppressed by the demon because of his own sins. The third cause is the negligence of those providing remedies; for in that instance, the "perfect men" were not present. Regarding this, John Chrysostom <note>An early Church Father and Archbishop of Constantinople known for his preaching</note> says that the pillars of faith—namely Peter, James, and John—were not there, just as they had been present at Christ’s Transfiguration; nor were prayer and fasting present there, without which that kind of demon is not at all
<margin>The unbelief of the Exorcist hinders the liberation of the possessed.</margin>
cured. The fourth cause is some flaw in the faith of the Exorcist; hence, when the disciples asked why they could not cast out the spirit, Christ replied: *Because of your unbelief; truly I say to you, if you have faith as a grain of mustard seed, you shall say to this mountain, "Move from here," and nothing will be impossible for you.* Where

<page-num>322</page-num>
<header>MOST EXCELLENT DOCTRINE <note>original: *DOCTR. PULCHERR.*; an abbreviation of *Doctrina Pulcherrima*</note></header>

<margin>The absence of an Exorcist ordained by God hinders the liberation of the possessed.</margin>
Saint Hilary <note>Hilary of Poitiers (c. 310–367 AD), a prominent bishop and theologian known for his works on the Trinity</note> says: "The disciples indeed believed, but they were not yet perfect in faith." For when the Lord delayed on the mountain with the other three, and they remained with the crowd, a certain sluggishness had loosened their faith. The fifth reason is so that the virtue existing in someone other than the Exorcist may be made known. Thus we read in the *Lives of the Fathers* <note>original: *Vitis Patrum*; a famous collection of the lives and sayings of the early desert monks</note> that possessed persons whom Saint Anthony <note>Saint Anthony the Great, the famous Egyptian monk</note> had not cured, were sometimes cured by Paul his disciple. The sixth reason is the purification of those who suffer the vexation <term>vexationem: the physical or mental harassment by a demon</term>; for often, even when the guilt of a sin is removed, the punishment is not removed, so that prior offenses may be purged away.

<margin>Those who cannot be freed are encouraged to patience.</margin>
Therefore, those who cannot be cured by the aforementioned lawful remedies, due to the hindrances described above, should be moved to patience and endurance for the forgiveness of their sins. They must be warned not to resort to unlawful remedies <note>This likely refers to consulting sorcerers, using magic charms, or other superstitious practices forbidden by the Church</note> against the will of God, lest worse things happen to them; as often happens to those who, not content to try lawful remedies, turn to the unlawful.

## Whether the possessed should be exorcised publicly or privately, and concerning the benefits that come to the faithful from the conjuration. Chapter XVIII.

<margin>Let the Exorcist beware of private association with a woman.</margin>
An Exorcist must take care that, during the time of the conjuration <term>conjuratio: the formal command or oath used by the priest to compel a demon to leave</term>, he does not remain alone with a woman, whether in a church or in a private place, even if the woman being exorcised is very old. This is because, first, demons sometimes shake human bodies so violently that unless others are present to hold the afflicted person, they can only be exorcised with the greatest difficulty; second, because an occasion for scandal could arise between the minister and the afflicted person; and third, because when devout persons are present praying for the victim, the demons yield more easily.

<margin>Where the possessed are to be exorcised.</margin>
As to whether the possessed should be exorcised publicly or privately in a church—that is, with the doors open or closed—it seems to me that there are as many opinions as there are heads. Yet I know this one thing: that the order of Exorcist is one of the seven holy orders of the Church, which performs nothing in secret but conducts everything publicly, and that our Lord Jesus Christ cast out de-

### ¶ The continuation of the Holy Gospel according to John.

<margin>Chapter 16.</margin>
At that time, Jesus said to his disciples: Amen, amen, I say to you, if you ask the Father anything in my name, he will give it to you. Until now, you have not asked for anything in my name. The hour is coming when I will no longer speak to you in figures, but I will tell you plainly about my Father. On that day you will ask in my name, and I do not say to you that I will ask the Father for you. For the Father himself loves you, because you have loved me and have believed that I came from God. I came from the Father and have come into the world; again, I am leaving the world and going to the Father. His disciples said to him: Behold, now you are speaking plainly and using no figures. Now we know that you know all things, and do not need anyone to question you. By this we believe that you came from God.

Lord God almighty, author of unconquered power, and King of an insuperable empire, and always a magnificent conqueror: you who suppress the forces of the opposing dominion; who powerfully defeat hostile wickednesses; who created all things from nothing through your word; who expelled Lucifer and his followers from the empyrean heaven <note>Empyrean: In medieval theology, the highest part of heaven, thought to be composed of pure fire or light.</note> as proud and rebellious ones; who through your Son reconciled the lowest things with the highest; <margin>Ezekiel 18</margin> you who do not wish the death of a sinner, nor rejoice in the perdition of the dying; whose nature it is always to have mercy and to spare: have mercy on this your creature [Name], <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen* (Name), indicating where the priest would insert the name of the person being exorcised.</note> looking not at his sins, but at your kind clemency, and deliver him from all the snares of evil spirits by which he is daily bound, and from every infestation and trouble from them by which he is tortured. <margin>Mark 16</margin> Just as you delivered Mary Magdalene from seven unclean spirits, and the daughter of the Canaanite woman from the vexation of a demon, so now deign to deliver this [Name] from all evil spirits infesting him. ### ->E X O R C I S M.<-

I exorcise you [Name] in the name of God the Fa†ther almighty, in the name of Jesus † Christ, his son our Lord, and in the power of the Holy † Spirit, that you may become a clean vessel, holy, and purged from every stain of iniquity, and from all sorceries, incantations, bindings, signs, and spells; <note>original: "maleficiis, incantationibus, ligationibus, signaturis, & facturis." Through him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

<header>EXORCISM I.</header>

### P R A Y E R.

<margin>From the *Malleus Maleficarum*, part 2, question 2, chapter 6.</margin> <note>The *Malleus Maleficarum* (1486) was a prominent treatise on the prosecution of witches; these prayers were used to distinguish between natural illness and supernatural affliction.</note>
O God of mercy, O gracious God, who according to the multitude of your mercies, you correct those whom you love, and you mercifully restrain for their improvement those whom you receive. We call upon you, O Lord, that you might deign to confer your grace upon your servant [Name] <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen* (Name), indicating where the practitioner would speak the name of the afflicted.</note> who suffers weakness of the limbs in his body; so that whatever has been corrupted by earthly frailty, and whatever has been violated by diabolical fraud, you may join as a redeemed member to the unity of the body of the Church. Have mercy, O Lord, on his groans, have mercy on his tears, and admit him to the <term>sacrament of reconciliation</term> <note>A reference to Penance or Confession, restoring the person to a state of grace.</note>, for he has no confidence except in your mercy. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

->*And let holy water be sprinkled over the possessed person.*<-

### E X O R C I S M,

<margin>From the *Malleus* as cited above.</margin>
I exorcise you, [Name], an infirm person, but one reborn by the water of holy baptism, by the living † God, by the true † God, by the holy † God, by the God who redeemed you by his precious blood, that you may become an exorcised man, so that every <term>illusion</term> <note>original: "phantasia." In this context, it refers to mental disturbances or sensory deceptions believed to be caused by demons.</note> and wickedness of diabolical fraud may flee and depart, and every unclean spirit be adjured. By Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, etc.

### P R A Y E R.

O God, who always rules over your creation with pious affection, incline your ear to our supplications, and look favorably upon your servant [Name] struggling with the ill health of his body, and visit him, and grant him the medicine of your salvation and heavenly grace. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

<page-num>336</page-num>
<header>THE WHIP OF DEMONS</header>

*And let holy water be sprinkled over the possessed person. Afterward, with great faith and hope, let the Exorcist begin to adjure the demons existing in the possessed body, saying:*

## ADJURATION.

<margin>From the Apostles' Creed.</margin> <note>The "Apostles' Creed" is a fundamental statement of Christian belief; the exorcist uses its narrative of Christ's life as a weapon against the demon.</note>
Listen, unclean spirit of the devil; I warn you, and I exorcise † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the practitioner makes the Sign of the Cross over the afflicted person.</note> you, and I command you, O tempter—vain, senseless, false, heretical, empty, enemy, drunkard, <term>whisperer</term> <note>original: *susurro*; in this context, it refers to a spirit that sows discord or secret malice</note>, foolish, cast down from the grace of God and of Christ. By Him I exorcise you †, who for our sake descended to earth, who was named by an Angel, and was incarnate by the Holy Spirit, born of the Virgin Mary, and grew in age and wisdom; who in His twelfth year came into the Temple, and sitting in the midst of the Doctors, questioned them wisely. I adjure you † by Him, who was baptized by John the Baptist in the river Jordan, was tempted by the devil, sold and betrayed by His disciple Judas, captured, mocked, scourged, fed with gall, given vinegar to drink, bound, and crowned with thorns, stripped of His clothes, for which garments they cast lots.

<margin>In the same place.</margin>
I conjure you † by Him, who was crucified, died, and was buried, and on the third day rose again from the dead and ascended into heaven, who sits at the right hand of God the Father, from whence He shall come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire: so that you may immediately depart and flee from this vessel and creature of God; and that you offend neither me nor those standing by, and that you leave without harming this person.

<margin>Luke 1.</margin>
I exorcise you † by Him, whom the Angel Gabriel announced in the womb of the Blessed Virgin Mary as the one to come, and whom John [the Baptist] greeted from within Elizabeth's womb; and by Him I conjure you, so that whatever thing I shall ask you, you shall speak and show me the truth: what your master is called, and what you are called; and whether you are in this body, or outside the body, or if you are alone, or a <term>legion</term> <note>A reference to Mark 5:9, where a group of demons identifies itself as "Legion, for we are many."</note> is with you, or many legions.

*Here the Exorcist may ask what the name of the possessing demon is: how many companions it has: what its master is called: under which power of demons*

<meta>The catchword "monum" at the bottom of the original page is the end of the word "daemonum" (of demons), linking to the first word of the next page.</meta>

<note>original: "monum sit"</note> what its name is: for what reason it oppresses the possessed: if it is bound there by any <term>evil spell</term> <note>original: *maleficio*; refers to harmful magic or a curse intended to "bind" a spirit to a victim</note>: how that spell might be broken: how long it has lived there: by which Saint it must depart: through which person: then, who are its greatest enemies in heaven, and who in hell: and what sign it will give upon its exit. Afterward, command it: that it must not harm the patient, and that it must not dare to return to them ever again, nor bring about a time of evil. But be prudent and circumspect in your questioning, lest you be deceived. For you should strive more to free God’s creature with the humility of mind which is necessary for this, rather than to speak with demons. Finally, once the questions have been asked, whether it has retreated or not, or whether it has answered or not, proceed with this conjuration.

<header>EXORCISM II.</header>

# EXORCISM II.
## AGAINST DEMONS
### possessing human bodies.

<margin>The Athanasian Creed <note>A statement of Christian belief focused on the Trinity and the Incarnation, traditionally used to combat heresy and spiritual error.</note></margin>
IN the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy † Spirit, Amen. Let God arise, and let his enemies be scattered: and let them that hate him flee from before his face. As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish: as wax melts before the fire, so let all demons perish from before our face.

->*Here let the Exorcist show the Cross to the possessed person, and say:*<-

BEhold the Cross † of our Lord Jesus Christ; flee, you hostile powers, for Christ wills to pursue you. The Father is uncreated, † the Son is uncreated, † the Holy Spirit is uncreated; † the Lion of the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, has conquered. Hail, holy Cross, through which such great gifts are given. Make me desire your will; drive away the things that are harmful to us. Grant me strength, pardon, peace, and salvation. Be my hope while I live, and my protection when I am dying. Behold the Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ; flee, you hostile powers, for Christ wills to pursue you. By this sign of the Cross, let God drive away every evil thing. Let all phantoms <note>original: "phantasmata." Deceptive spirits, apparitions, or mental illusions sent by the devil.</note> depart, so they may not harm the mind. Good Cross, worthy Cross, wood above all woods; Cross, the way of virtue; Cross, the way of true salvation; Cross, the strength of men; the Cross lifts us to the Lord.

->*Here let the Exorcist say while kneeling:*<-

<margin>Gen. 1.</margin>
ALmighty Lord, creator of heaven and earth, Jesus Christ, King of kings, supreme power, governing all things justly with holy guidance. Coming from the heavens, born of the holy Virgin. The hand of the almighty Ruler of Olympus <note>original: "Olympi." A poetic, classical reference used here to mean the highest Heaven or the dwelling of God.</note> is revealed on earth. After you gave holy miracles in a thousand ways. Fastened to the Cross, you saved with your blood <margin>Mark 16.</margin>

## CONJURATION.

<margin>Saint Jerome <note>A 4th-century Church Father known for translating the Bible into Latin; his writings were often cited in exorcism manuals for their authority on spiritual matters.</note></margin>
**I** conjure you therefore, O demon, by the living † God, in the power of the Holy Spirit: and by him who created you and cast you down from heaven, and by the fearsome day of Judgment: and by the ineffable name of the Lord: that you tell me who you are, and what you are called. And by these holy names of God, at my command, you must depart without delay from this creature of God, N. <note>A placeholder for the name of the possessed individual (Latin: "nomen").</note> without bringing any pain upon them: nor doing any harm, immediately, and without injury to any person. Hel <note>original: "Hel." A Hebrew name for God, meaning "The Strong One."</note> † God <note>original: "Heloym." The plural of majesty for God in Hebrew.</note> † My Lord <note>original: "Adonay."</note> † Almighty <note>original: "Saday."</note> † Savior <note>original: "Sother." A Greek title for Christ.</note> † God with us <note>original: "Emanuel."</note> † The Four-Letter Name <note>original: "Thetragrammaton." The unutterable name of God, YHWH.</note> † Alpha † & Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, signifying the totality of God.</note> † The Beginning & The End † Holy † Strong † O God † Immortal <note>original: "Agios, Yschiros, Otheos, Athanatos." A Greek theological term meaning "of the same essence," referring to the Trinity.</note> † Lord † Jesus † Christ † Messiah † God † I Am Who I Am. <note>original: "Eheye." The name God revealed to Moses at the burning bush.</note> Again I conjure you † O demon, by the living † God, by the true † God, by the Holy † God, by the Fa † ther, by the † Son, and by the Holy † Spirit: by the Blessed Virgin Mary: by the entire heavenly court, and by all things through which you can be compelled and bound in the name of Jesus: that you suddenly manifest yourself, O wicked spirit, and answer me concerning whatever thing I shall ask you.

# THE SCOURGE OF DEMONS

> ...marveled. <note>Continuing from the previous page: "and the crowds marveled."</note> I cast out demons by Beelzebub <note>original: "Belzebub." A name derived from a Philistine deity, used in the Gospels to refer to the prince of demons.</note>, by whom do your sons cast them out? Therefore they themselves shall be your judges. Furthermore, if I cast out demons by the finger of God, surely the kingdom of God has come upon you. When a strong man, well-armed, guards his courtyard, all that he possesses is in peace. But if one stronger than he comes upon him and overcomes him, he will take away all his weapons in which he trusted, and distribute his spoils. He who is not with me is against me. And he who does not gather with me, scatters. When the unclean spirit has gone out of a man, he walks through dry places, seeking rest, and not finding it, he says: "I will return to my house from which I came out." And when he comes, he finds it swept with brooms and decorated. And then he goes and takes with him seven other spirits more wicked than himself, and entering they dwell there. And the final state of that man becomes worse than the first. It happened, however, while he said these things, a certain woman from the crowd, lifting her voice, said to him: "Blessed is the womb that bore you, and the breasts that you sucked." But he said: "Rather, blessed are those who hear the word of God and keep it." ℞. Praise to you, O Christ.

### ¶ The following of the Holy Gospel according to Luke.

<margin>Chapter 13.</margin>
At that time, Jesus spoke this parable to the crowds. "A certain man had a fig tree planted in his vineyard: and he came seeking fruit on it, and found none. And he said to the dresser of the vineyard: 'Behold, for three years now I have come seeking fruit on this fig tree, and I find none. Cut it down therefore; why should it even take up space in the ground?' But he, answering, said to him: 'Lord, let it alone this year also, until I dig around it and apply manure. And if it bears fruit, well; but if not, then in the future you shall cut it down.'" Now He was teaching in their Synagogue on the Sabbaths. And behold, there was a woman who had a spirit of infirmity <term>spiritum infirmitatis: a phrase used to describe an illness of demonic or spiritual origin</term> for eighteen years: and she was bent over, and was not able to look upwards at all. When Jesus saw her, he called her to himself and said to her: "Woman, you are released from your infirmity." And he laid his hands upon her: and immediately she was made straight, and she glorified God. But the leader of the synagogue <note>original: "Archisynagogus"</note>, indignant because Jesus had healed on the Sabbath, responding, said to the crowd: "There are six days in which it is proper to work; come on those days and be healed, and not on the Sabbath day." But the Lord, answering him, said: "Hypocrites, does not each one of you on the Sabbath loosen his ox or his donkey from the stall and lead it to water? And should not this daughter of Abraham, whom Satan has bound—behold—for eighteen years, be loosed from this bond on the Sabbath day?" And when he said these things, all his adversaries were ashamed: and all the people rejoiced in all the glorious things that were done by him. ℞. Praise to you, O Christ.

### CONJURATION.

<margin>From ancient conjurations.</margin> Listen then, most malignant demon, listen damned creature, rejected and cursed by God; listen, I say, to your own pride. You have forsaken the God who brought you forth, and you have forgotten the Lord your Creator; who, because of your pride, handed you over to eternal fires along with your prince Satan and the other demons. In the strength and power of this God and Creator of all—trusting not in my own merits or strength, but in His—you cursed devil and most impure Satan, and most foul spirit: you who, because of the perversity of your choice, despised and abandoned <margin>Isaiah 14</margin> the dwelling of the Almighty Lord our God. He exists as the Three and One God before the ages, and He is the cause of existence for every creature, such that the world could not stand for the blink of an eye if He withdrew His governance. You chose to contradict Him, and you refused to set yourself in order and turn to Him, nor to obey Him faithfully, just as those most blessed spirits <note>The "blessed spirits" refer to the angels who remained loyal to God during the rebellion of Lucifer.</note> did, who enjoy the fullness of His happiness. <margin>In the same place. Jude, single chapter. <note>The Epistle of Jude consists of only one chapter; it is frequently cited in exorcisms for its description of the fall of the rebel angels.</note></margin> Instead, in the perverse order of pride and envy, you took pleasure in yourself; and in yourself and in the blinded angels you placed your lot; because of which impurity of your choice, you were cast...

Simon Magus was a sorcerer mentioned in the Book of Acts who attempted to buy the power of the Holy Spirit, becoming a symbol for heresy and illicit magic.</note>; and just as through the blessed Apostle *Bartholomew* he healed all those wounded by the demon inhabiting the idol *Astaroth* <note>Astaroth is a name derived from the Phoenician goddess Astarte, but in medieval and Renaissance demonology, he was characterized as a powerful Duke of Hell.</note>. And through the <margin>Exodus 7</margin> holy prophet *Moses*, who exposed the spells of the Egyptian magicians *Jannes* and *Mambres* <note>In Jewish and Christian tradition, these are the names given to the sorcerers of Pharaoh's court who opposed Moses.</note> by the finger of the almighty God; so now, by that same finger of God—namely, the Holy Spirit, who proceeds from the Father and his Son—we dissolve all sorcerous arts and spells cast upon this servant *N.* by the atrocity of your envy, through which you, malignant and unclean spirit, lie in wait for human salvation according to your impious will: whether by the just dispensation of our almighty Lord God, or even by the requirement of his own sins or those of his parents. Between these I interpose the abundant redemption of the most precious blood of our Lord *Jesus Christ*, who loved us and washed us <margin>Revelation 1</margin> from our sins in his own blood. Who, coming into this world, conquered your prince, not only by power but by the price of his most precious blood; and he bound that proud one, broke his vessels, plundering principalities and powers, and snatched us justly and powerfully from your power. ...and our Head, ascending into heaven, gave power to his disciples and his faithful to cast you out from the bodies of men, saying: *In my name they shall cast out demons.* <margin>Mark 16</margin> And elsewhere he said to the twelve Apostles and disciples: *I have given you power and authority over all demons, and over the power of the enemy* <note>original: "Dedi vobis virtutē, & potestatem supra omnia dæmonia, & virtutem inimici." These are the words of Jesus in Luke 10:19, central to the legalistic "authorization" of the exorcist.</note>; of whose number he willed us to be, though we are unworthy. For through our ministry at the table of the altar, he transubstantiates <term>transubstantiates: the miraculous transformation of the essence of bread and wine into the body and blood of Christ during the Mass</term> the substance of bread and wine into his body and blood. 

In this power and authority granted to me by Almighty God and our Lord Jesus Christ, I confront you, unclean spirit—whether you are one or many—however you have presumed to rashly enter into this servant of God, *N.* <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen* (Name), indicating where the priest would speak the specific name of the person being exorcised.</note>, who has been sealed and initiated into the divine mysteries; whether through food, or drink, or in any wicked superstition, or by any spells whatsoever performed upon this servant of God. And I command you in the name of God the Fa † ther Almighty, of his Son † our Lord Jesus Christ, and of the Holy † Spirit, that you depart and withdraw from this servant of God, *N.*, without any harm to him; so that henceforth you may not presume to approach him, nor dwell in him for even a moment, nor disturb his internal or external senses, from the sole of his foot to the crown of his head; nor stir up his humors <term>humors: in pre-modern medicine, the four bodily fluids (blood, phlegm, black bile, and yellow bile) thought to govern health and personality; demons were believed to manipulate these to cause madness or sickness</term>; nor lead him into wasting away, nor vex him with any infirmity; nor hinder his rest during the hours of day or night. 

But if you stubbornly despise obeying our commands and sentences—or rather, more truly, those of God and our Lord Jesus Christ, our supreme and most invincible Emperor—we humbly beseech the same God and our Lord Jesus Christ that, through the power of his most sacred passion, and through the merits of the most blessed Virgin Mary, his blessed mother, and of the holy Angels who see the revealed face of God the Father Almighty; and through the merits of our fathers the blessed Patriarchs, Prophets, Apostles, Martyrs, Confessors, and holy Virgins, that...

<summary>This page outlines the spiritual authority of the exorcist, derived from Christ’s commission to the Apostles and the power of the Eucharist, while delivering a detailed command for the demon to leave the victim without causing physical or mental harm.</summary>

# PRAYER.

> All Holy Angels, and Archangels, Thrones, Dominions, Principalities, and Powers, Virtues of the heavens, Cherubim, and Seraphim, who do not cease to cry out before God, saying: Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord God of Hosts <note>original: "Sabaoth," a Hebrew title for God meaning "of armies" or "of heavenly hosts."</note>, pour out holy prayers for me, a fragile sinner, before the most high God and our Lord, that He may have mercy on me, and mercifully and kindly forgive my sins; that He may free me, protect me, defend me, and snatch me away from all my temptations, and so make me pass through temporal goods that I may not lose the joys of eternal life. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

# PRAYER.

> O Most High Michael the Archangel, O prince of the heavenly host, O illustrious and supreme beauty, and minister who stands vigilantly before God; I ask that by your prayers you might favor me, a wretched mortal; so that by the mercy of the holy Lord and through your intercession, I may deserve to be snatched from demonic deception, and through you deserve to be presented before the majesty of God. Amen.

# PRAYER.

> God of the Angels, God of the Archangels, God of the Prophets, God of the Apostles, God of the Martyrs, God of the Virgins, God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, I invoke your name, and as a humble suppliant I beg the clemency of your illustrious majesty, that you may deign to provide me with help against this most wicked spirit. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

# EXORCISM III.

## PSALM.

> <margin>Psalm 90 <note>In modern Bibles, this is Psalm 91. It is the primary prayer used for protection against evil.</note></margin>He who dwells in the aid of the Most High shall abide under the protection of the God of heaven.
> He shall say to the Lord: You are my protector and my refuge; my God, in him will I trust.
> For he will deliver me from the snare of the hunters, and from the harsh word.
> He will overshadow you with his shoulders, and under his wings you shall find hope.
> His truth shall encompass you with a shield; you shall not be afraid of the terror of the night.
> Nor of the arrow that flies by day, nor of the trouble that wanders in the darkness, nor of the assault and the <term>noonday demon</term>. <note>original: "dæmonio meridiano." In early Christian thought, this "midday devil" was a spirit of spiritual exhaustion (acedia) or a sudden, overt demonic attack.</note>
> A thousand shall fall at your side, and ten thousand at your right hand; but it shall not come near you.
> Yet with your eyes you shall behold, and see the reward of the wicked.
> Because you, O Lord, are my hope; you have made the Most High your refuge.
> No evil shall come to you, nor shall any scourge approach your dwelling.
> For he has given his angels charge over you, to keep you in all your ways.
> In their hands they shall bear you up, lest perhaps you dash your foot against a stone.
> You shall walk upon the asp and the <term>basilisk</term>; <note>The basilisk was a mythical serpent king often used in these texts to represent the power of the devil.</note> you shall trample underfoot the lion and the dragon.
> Because he has hoped in me, I will deliver him; I will protect him, because he has known my name.
> He shall cry to me, and I will hear him; I am with him in tribulation; I will deliver him, and I will glorify him.
> I will fill him with length of days, and I will show him my salvation.
> Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, etc.

Amen.

# FOURTH EXORCISM
## TERRIBLE AND OF THE GREATEST efficacy against demons oppressing human bodies.

*The exorcist, when about to approach the act of exorcising, having first performed <term>sacramental confession: the ritual of confessing sins to a priest to receive absolution, ensuring the exorcist is in a state of grace</term>: kneeling with a pure and devoted heart before the image of the Crucifix, shall say the <term>Psalm: Here referring to Psalm 51 (Psalm 50 in the Vulgate), the "Miserere," a classic prayer of repentance</term>.*

Have mercy on me, O God: according to your great mercy, etc. [to be said] in its entirety.

V. Cleanse me from my hidden faults, O Lord.
R. And spare your servant from the sins of others.
V. Lord, hear my prayer.
R. And let my cry come unto you.
V. The Lord be with you.
R. And with your spirit.

->P R A Y E R.<-

<margin>The Roman Church.</margin>
O God, whose nature it is always to have mercy and to spare, receive our petition: so that the compassion of your kindness may graciously absolve me, and this your servant, whom the chain of sins binds. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

It moves from the scriptural precedent of casting out "Legion" into swine to a direct conjuration of the spirits using a series of powerful divine names.</meta>

<page-num>373</page-num>
<header>EXORCISM IV.</header>
<sig>Aa 3</sig>

"My name is Legion, for we are many." And he begged him earnestly not to drive them out of the region. Now there was a large herd of swine feeding near the mountain in the fields. And the spirits begged him, saying, "Send us into the swine, that we may enter into them." And Jesus immediately permitted them. And the unclean spirits, coming out, entered into the swine; and the herd rushed down the steep bank into the sea, about two thousand in number, and they were drowned in the sea. But those who fed them fled, and reported it in the city and in the fields. And the people went out to see what had happened; they came to Jesus and saw the man who had been troubled by the demon sitting there, clothed and in his right mind, and they were afraid. And those who had seen it told them how it had happened to the one who had the demon, and about the swine. And they began to beg him to depart from their borders. And as he was getting into the boat, the man who had been troubled by the demon began to beg him that he might be with him. But Jesus did not allow him, but said to him, "Go home to your people, and announce to them how great things the Lord has done for you, and how he has had mercy on you." And he went away and began to proclaim in the Decapolis <note>A group of ten cities on the eastern frontier of the Roman Empire.</note> how great things Jesus had done for him; and everyone marveled. ℞. Praise to you, O Christ.

->*Having completed these things, the Exorcist shall approach the possessed person, and signing him on the forehead with the cross, he shall say:*<-

<page-num>381</page-num>
<header>EXORCISM IV.</header>

->PRAYER.<-

<margin>From the conju-
rations of
St. Zeno.</margin> <note>original: "S. Zenonis." Saint Zeno of Verona (d. 371/372 AD) was a bishop known for his sermons on the power of Christ over demons and his role in converting the city.</note>

> O Lord, Almighty, God, <note>original: "Adonay, Saday, Eloym." These are Latinized versions of Hebrew names for God: Adonai (Lord), Shaddai (Almighty), and Elohim (God).</note> by the invocation of Your most holy name, Lord, holy Father, almighty, eternal God, grant us Your help over the smoke of this incense; so that it may be our aid in driving out those cursed spirits who take hold of this, Your image and likeness. <note>A reference to the possessed person, who, according to Genesis, is made in the image and likeness of God.</note> Just as the smoke of spices rises in Your sight, Lord Jesus Christ; so may the smoke of this incense torture, ignite, and expel the aforementioned most wicked and abominable spirits, and through all the powers of the heavens, grant us that these same evil spirits no longer have any power over this creature [Name] <note>original: "N." An abbreviation for 'Nomen' (Name), indicating where the exorcist should speak the name of the afflicted person.</note> redeemed by Your precious blood. Who live and reign forever and ever, Amen.

---

->EXORCISM.<-

> <margin>Sirach 44.</margin> <note>original: "Eccles. 44." ## CONJURATION.

<margin>Luke 1</margin>
I adjure † <note>The cross symbol indicates that the priest would make the sign of the cross over the possessed person at this moment.</note> you, demon, by that venerable Annunciation which the Angel Gabriel made to the Virgin Mary, saying: <margin>Luke 1</margin> "Hail Mary, full of grace, the Lord is with you." And by the humble and prudent response with which she spoke to the Angel: <margin>In the same place</margin> "Behold the handmaid of the Lord, let it be done to me according to your word." ## CONJURATION.

I adjure † <note>The cross symbol indicates that the exorcist should make the sign of the cross over the afflicted person.</note> you all spirits who infest this creature of God, [Name], by whatever name you are called, and all those favoring you, of whatever rank <margin>Luke 1</margin> they may be, that as a minister of our Lord Jesus Christ, having full authority, freedom, and power, I now most strictly enjoin, mandate, and command you all through these most holy names of God: Hel † Elohim <note>original: "Heloym"</note> † Adonai <note>original: "Adonay"</note> † Shaddai <note>original: "Saday"</note> † Yah <note>original: "Ya"</note> † Jehovah † Agla <note>A Kabbalistic acronym for the Hebrew "Ata Gebir Leilam Adonai," meaning "You are mighty forever, O Lord."</note> † Emmanuel † Holy <note>original: "Agyos"</note> † God <note>original: "Otheos"</note> † Mighty <note>original: "Ischyros"</note> † Immortal <note>original: "Arthanatos"</note> † Alpha, and Omega,
<margin>Isaiah 6</margin> Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord God of Hosts <note>original: "Sabaoth"</note>, Father, Word, and Holy Spirit, Most Holy Trinity, One God, Creator of all: that immediately, without delay, and with every excuse removed, all you spirits infesting and vexing this image of God <note>A reference to the theological concept that humans are made in the image of God (Imago Dei).</note>, and dwelling within it from whatever place, wherever you lie hidden, you must depart and ascend upwards to the tongue, not to leave without my permission: and speak the truth with me concerning everything that I wish to ask you, openly and clearly, so that I may understand you well: and from now on I command you by all...

...and you, cursed devil, and the world through fire, so that you immediately go out and withdraw from this servant of God, N. <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen*, indicating where the priest would insert the name of the person being exorcised.</note> with all your iniquities, spells, incantations, bindings, and sorceries, never to return to him. ## CONJURATION.

Therefore, let our Lord Jesus Christ, the Son of God, compel you; let the Angels of God compel you; let the Archangels compel you; let the Patriarchs and Prophets compel you; let the Apostles and Evangelists compel you; let the Martyrs compel you; let the Confessors compel you; let the Virgins compel you; let all the Saints and elect of God compel you; let all your arts and snares fail day by day and at every hour. Therefore, O rebels against God, return strength [to the person]; return health to the soul; do not tempt them, nor hand them over to death: neither in food, nor in drink, nor in the places where they live; do not come upon them while they are awake, nor while they are sleeping; do not harm them, nor suffocate their voice, nor hinder them as they seek eternal life.

Depart, therefore, by the name of the eternal God, from the head, from the hair, from the crown of the head, from the forehead, from the eyes, from the tongue, from under the tongue, from the ears, from the nostrils, from the neck, from the jaws, from the teeth, from the throat, from the gums, from the mouth, from the palate, from the brain, from the membranes of the brain, from the eyelashes and eyebrows, from the body hair, from the feet, from the shins, from the knees, from the legs, from the private parts, from the kidneys, from the sides, from the upper and lower intestines, from the thigh, from the belly, from the stomach, from the heart, from the shoulder blades, from the shoulders, from the chest, from the breasts, from the arms, from the hands, from the nails, from the bones, from the nerves, from the veins, from the marrow, from the lungs, from the structure of his limbs, from all joints, and from the whole body, and from all parts of the body, and from his garments, both inside and out, and from all the flesh, from all the skin, wherever you lie hidden, or are able to hide: separate yourselves from this creature of God, N. <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen*, the place where the exorcist inserts the name of the person being treated.</note> and just as the Lord God separated heaven from earth, light from darkness, truth from falsehood, <margin>Genesis 1</margin> and good from evil: so may you be separated from this creature of God, N., you most unclean spirits, now and forever. <summary>This text concludes the fifth rite of exorcism with an exhaustive anatomical command, ordering the demon to vacate every specific organ, limb, and even the clothing of the possessed person. This reflects the medieval and early modern belief that evil spirits could physically hide within the minutiae of human anatomy.</summary>

Create a clean heart in me, O God: and renew a right 
spirit within my bowels <note>original: "visceribus meis." In early modern anatomical and spiritual understanding, the "bowels" or "inwards" were considered the seat of deep emotion and the soul.</note>.
Cast me not away from your face: and take not your
Holy Spirit from me.
Restore to me the joy of your salvation: and strengthen 
me with a guiding spirit.
I will teach the unjust your ways: and the wicked 
shall be converted to you.
Deliver me from blood-guilt, O God, the God of my 
salvation: and my tongue shall extol your justice.
O Lord, you will open my lips: and my mouth shall 
declare your praise.
For if you had desired sacrifice, I would indeed have 
given it: you will not be delighted with burnt offerings.
A sacrifice to God is a troubled spirit: a contrite and 
humbled heart, O God, you will not despise.
Deal favorably, O Lord, in your good will with Zion: 
that the walls of Jerusalem may be built up.
Then you shall accept the sacrifice of justice, 
oblations, and whole-burnt offerings: then they shall 
lay calves upon your altar.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, etc. <note>The "Gloria Patri," a standard doxology used to conclude the recitation of a Psalm.</note>

### P R A Y E R.

Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

O Lord God, holy Father Almighty, who are the Alpha and Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing God as the beginning and the end of all things.</note>, that is, the beginning and the end. I reverently and mercifully seek my God and Lord, and I humbly supplicate you as much as I know how and am able, so that you might now deign to let me be in your holy service and the ministry of this art <note>original: "hujus artis." ->¶ The Continuation of the Holy Gospel according to Luke.<-

<margin>Chapter 9.</margin>
At 그 time, having called the twelve Apostles together, Jesus gave them power and authority over all demons, and to cure diseases. And he sent them to preach the kingdom of God, and to heal the sick. And going out, they went through the towns preaching the gospel and healing everywhere.

<header>THE SCOURGE OF DEMONS</header>
<meta>catchword: cur.</meta>

# CONJURATION OF THE FIRE TO
## burn the image of the demon
## painted on paper.

*Here the Exorcist should have a painted image of that demon who is oppressing the possessed person, prepared with the demon's name written above the head of the image itself: and while conjuring the fire, which has been similarly prepared, he shall say the following conjuration.*

## CONJURATION.

<margin>Exodus 3</margin>
I conjure you, † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the Sign of the Cross over the fire.</note> O fire, by God the Father almighty, who appeared to Moses in the form of fire, and by Him who delivered the three youths from the furnace of burning fire <note>original: "tres pueros de camino ignis ardentis." This refers to the Biblical story of Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego in the Book of Daniel, who were thrown into a furnace but remained unharmed.</note>, and by all the holy burnt offerings which have been made in you and from you to the praise of Almighty God, my great Creator and yours; and by Him whose seat is fire, and His dwelling is fire, and He Himself is fire, and His surroundings are fire, and before whom fire dwells, and from whose side flames of fire go forth, who created all fires; and a devouring fire shall go out before Him, and shall strike and burn all those who ultimately contradict Him, do not obey His words, and do not perform His will. I conjure you, † O fire, by holy Mary, the glorious mother of our Lord Jesus Christ: and by Saint John the Baptist, and by all the holy Angels and Archangels, by the Thrones and Dominations, Principalities and Powers, and by the Virtues, Cherubim and Seraphim; by the Patriarchs and Prophets: and by all the Apostles and Evangelists; and by all the holy Disciples and Innocents of our Lord Jesus Christ: and by all the Martyrs and Confessors <note>In this context, "Confessors" are saints who lived lives of heroic virtue and suffered for the faith, but were not killed as martyrs.</note>: and by all the holy Virgins and Widows; and by all the Saints of God, both men and women: and by the entire heavenly Court: and by these names of God: **Agla** <note>A Hebrew acronym for *Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai*, meaning "You, O Lord, are mighty forever." It was frequently used in medieval and Renaissance magic and exorcism.</note>, **Tetragrammaton** <note>A Greek term meaning "four letters," referring to the sacred four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>, **Jehovah**, and by this name **Jesus**, and by the name of God, who powerfully rules over all fire—

<summary>This page contains a specialized ritual for burning a demon's image to break its influence over a possessed person, invoking God through various biblical references to fire and the celestial hierarchy.</summary>
<keywords>fire ritual, sympathetic magic, celestial hierarchy, Agla, Tetragrammaton, exorcism procedure, Shadrach Meshach and Abednego</keywords>

--- Page 131 ---
<meta>This page continues the ritual action from page 414. Having prepared the fire and the symbolic image of the demon, the exorcist now performs the "sympathetic magic" of the rite—commanding that the physical heat and destruction of the paper be reflected as spiritual agony upon the demon inhabiting the victim.</meta>

<page-num>415</page-num>
<header>EXORCISM VI.</header>

...[con]jured. Again, and for the last time, I conjure you † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the Sign of the Cross over the fire.</note> O fire, by Him <margin>Psalm 103</margin> <note>original: "Psal. 103." Referring to the verse "He looks on the earth and it trembles."</note> who makes the world tremble, that immediately and with the greatest fury—being adjured by so many and such most holy words of your Creator—you may powerfully heat this paper <note>original: "schedulam," referring to the slip of paper or parchment containing the demon's image.</note>, in which there is a diabolical image. This image is presently assigned to represent the form and likeness of this most wicked spirit; on this paper his name is also written above the head of the figure itself.

And you, O adjured fire, shall horribly and speedily burn it when I cast and place it into you. You shall terribly burn that paper and the image assigned to this most wicked spirit. Just as you, O fire, gradually heat and burn this paper with the image and his name, let that spirit—who exists unlawfully in this vessel <note>The "vessel" refers to the body of the possessed person.</note>—feel the heat within his entire substance and within himself, a thousand times more from day to day. May he always and horribly feel this heat until the Day of Judgment, with this punishment and pain always increasing a hundredfold from hour to hour. 

No other spirit, of whatever condition, power, or virtue they may be, shall dare to take this pain away from him, nor obey him, nor give him any help, by the virtue of God and the words I have spoken. By their power, this spirit, [Name], <note>The exorcist would speak the specific name of the demon here.</note> shall be unable to resist this punishment in any way, but shall instead withdraw from this body. Defeated by this pain, let him go out; and being most strictly compelled, let him withdraw from this creature of God, [Name], <note>The name of the possessed person.</note> immediately. He is not to return to them, and he must leave without injury to the body or any other harm, as I have commanded him above. 

This punishment shall not be lifted until he first departs and goes into the deep abyss <margin>Matthew 25</margin> <note>original: "Mat. 25." Referring to the "eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels."</note>. From there, he shall have no power to leave until the Day of Judgment. Let there be no one who is able to undo these things which I have said, and which I say and do, by the power of Him who is coming to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

# BLESSING OF THE FIRE.

℣. Our help is in the name of the Lord.
℟. Who made heaven and earth.
℣. The Lord be with you.
℟. And with your spirit.

### P R A Y E R.

Bless, † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the Sign of the Cross over the fire.</note> O Lord God Almighty, this creature <note>In liturgical Latin, "creature" refers to any created thing or element, such as fire, water, or salt.</note> of fire, with your heavenly † blessing, to drive away and to smoke out <note>original: "fumigandum." This suggests the fire may have been used with incense or foul-smelling substances to drive out the spirit.</note> this spirit dwelling around this image of yours <note>Here, "image" refers to the possessed person, who is created in the image of God.</note>, or within this image of yours, and all those favoring him: and to drive away and repel the aforementioned spirit from this your creature [Name] <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder for the name of the person being exorcised.</note>, and to send him into the place where the fire shall not die, along with all those favoring him, without harm to this creature, or to any other creatures. You, O Lord, who can do all things, may you sanctify by sanctifying, and bless by blessing this creature of fire. Let the punishment become horrible from hour to hour, great and most powerful to drive away and to subjugate the aforementioned most wicked spirit—whether he wills it or not—from this your image [Name], wherever he lurks, into the depth of the abyss with all those favoring him, and without harm to any [other] creature. May he go out from her, never to return, neither he nor any other to her. Let this punishment, in its very substance and in its entirety, begin cruelly to take effect, and let him feel it powerfully when I place and cast this painted image <note>Referring back to the drawing of the demon mentioned on page 415.</note> into this creature of fire to be burned; and let it grow and always be increased most atrociously from hour to hour a thousandfold after I place the aforementioned image to be burned and it has been consumed. To this punishment may the aforementioned spirit be unable to resist, so that he departs, never to return, as I have said; which punishment shall not be taken away unless he first departs with all the aforesaid, in your name, Jesus Christ the Nazarene, the Crucified, who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire, and the damned spirit.
<margin>Psalm</margin> Amen. As wax melts <note>original: "deficit," meaning to fail, melt, or waste away.</note> before the face of fire, and dust before the face of the wind: so let all his power and virtue fail further...

### P R A Y E R.

O Glorious and most holy Adonay <note>original: "Adonay." This is a Hebrew title for God, meaning "Lord," used here to invoke His supreme authority.</note>, through whom all created things are governed and sustained; be favorably present at my invocations, and mercifully grant and concede that, by your power and the gift of your grace, this present fire may become a blessed creature <note>In this context, "creature" refers to the element of fire as a part of God's creation.</note> now and forever. Then the Exorcist, holding the painted image of the demon with its name over the fire, shall read the following adjuration.*

## REPROACHES AGAINST THE EVIL SPIRITS.

HEAR, hear, hear, [Name] <note>The letter "N." in the original text stands for the demon's name.</note>, you spirit cursed by God and cast down forever into eternal punishment! You most unspeakable, most wicked rebel against God, father of lies, betrayer of all, root of all evils, origin of discord, fuel of vices, foolish, senseless, whisperer <note>original: "susurrator." This refers to a deceptive spirit who whispers temptations or slanders.</note>, heretic, apostate, most filthy, most criminal, most foul-smelling, empty of all good and full of every evil! You murderer, reprobate, son of perdition and of eternal curse, enemy of the human race, robber of life, shunner of justice, origin of greed, most vile serpent, most ravenous wolf!

To Lucifer: <note>The exorcist addresses the high prince of demons to command all subordinate spirits under his hierarchy.</note> let there be no one who obeys you through these names of God, and in their virtue and power: **Adonai**, **Agla**, **Jesus**, **Tetragrammaton**, **Sabaoth**, **Messiah**, **Soter**, **Emmanuel**, **Agios**, **Otheos**, **Ischyros**, **Athanatos**, <note>original: "Adonay Agla, Jesus, Thetragrammaton, Sabaoth, Messias, Sother, Emanuel, Agyos, Orcheos, Yschyros, Athanatos." These are ancient titles for God derived from Hebrew and Greek. Respectively: My Lord; a Hebrew acronym for "Thou art mighty forever, O Lord"; the four-letter name of God (YHWH); God of Hosts; Anointed One; Savior; God with us; Holy; O God; Strong; and Immortal.</note> and through all other terrible and ineffable names of God. Since you have been rebellious against such great and most sacred names and mysteries of God, may the curse of Almighty God the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and the wrath and indignation of the Blessed Virgin Mary, and of all the holy Angels, and of Saint John the Baptist, and of the entire heavenly Court, and of all God's creatures marked with the sign of the Holy Cross, come and descend swiftly upon you, and upon Lucifer, Beelzebub, Satan, Astaroth, Behemoth, Berith, <note>A list of high-ranking demons often found in medieval and Renaissance grimoires.</note> and all the princes of demons, and demons of whatever condition and power they may be, 

<margin>Regarding these names, see chapter 2 of *The Scourge*.</margin>
if they should give you, most wicked spirit, any assistance; and may there be no one who obeys you or them, through all the things I have said and am about to say, and henceforth always in the virtue and power of all the things I have named above, and in the name, virtue, and power of our great Lord, the almighty, strong, and terrible Jesus Christ of Nazareth, crucified for us wretched sinners. Thus may all your power be broken and bound indissolubly, along with the power of any other spirit favoring you and resisting the divine words and my commands—or rather, the commands of Jesus Christ, the Son of Almighty God, whose authority and office I now perform, though I am unworthy and a wretched sinner, and insufficient in my own fragility and lack of virtue.

<margin>John 18</margin>

<margin>Matthew 27</margin>
the veil of the Temple was torn in two parts from top to bottom, and many bodies of the Saints who had slept arose, so may all the things I have said, and will say, and am about to do, come upon you with effect. Amen.

<meta>catchword: nomen (name)</meta>

<margin>Psalm 23 <note>In the Vulgate Bible, Psalm 23 (24 in modern bibles) contains the command "Lift up your heads, O ye gates," which was traditionally interpreted as Christ breaking the gates of Hell during the Harrowing of Hell.</note></margin>
[Chri]st <note>original: "stus"; the start of the word 'Christus' was on the previous page</note> through his death broke the gates of hell; and from this moment, let all your power and strength be bound by all the things I have spoken, O most wicked spirit, you who dwell unlawfully in this body, just as Satan was bound by the Angel <margin>Revelation 20</margin>: and just as the incantations of Simon <note>Simon Magus, a sorcerer who challenged the Apostle Peter in Rome; he is often cited in exorcisms as the archetype of failed magic.</note> failed and were reduced to nothing in the sight of the Roman people through the prayers of the blessed Apostle Peter <margin>From the Itinerary of Clement <note>The *Clementine Recognitions*, an early Christian work detailing the travels of Peter and his conflict with Simon Magus.</note></margin>, so by the power that cast you down from heaven, let all chains, bindings, and incantations—of whatever condition or strength they may be—suddenly and immediately fail and come to nothing. 

And just as the most blessed holy Virgin Mary and all the virgins of God rejoice with Christ in eternal glory, so may you, immediately through the name of our Lord Jesus Christ of Nazareth, the Crucified, and by the power of his passion, depart suddenly from this creature of God, [Name] <note>The letter 'N.' stands for 'Nomen,' the place where the exorcist inserts the name of the afflicted person.</note>, and go into the depths of the abyss with an evident sign, without injury to them or to any of those standing by: in that manner and on that condition which I commanded you above, with all your chains, bindings, and incantations broken, even if they stand in the way of your departure. 

And hear now, most wicked spirit, and henceforth do not dare to stay nor remain in this vessel <note>The human body is frequently referred to as a "vessel" (vas) for the soul or the Holy Spirit.</note> and creation of God, wherever you are hiding, whether within or without: by the power of all things I have named, I powerfully command you and most strictly order you, by the authority of our Lord Jesus Christ. Flee, flee, you most vile rebel against God, and against His names and all holy words. Give way, most stubborn one, give way, most shameless one, give way to Jesus Christ: depart suddenly, most vile spirit, and let there be no one who obeys or favors you. Otherwise, I will repeat everything named up to this point, to the praise of God and to your confusion, and I will cast you into the lake of fire and sulfur to be burned eternally; I condemn you most strictly until the day of judgment, and I will curse you powerfully and terribly, and I will anathematize <term>anathematize: to pronounce a formal ecclesiastical curse or excommunication; here, it is a spiritual banishment</term> you, and I will bind you most strictly in the lower hell, where there shall be no rest for you forever. O most cruel serpent and most wicked devourer, the Word of God the Father comes from his holy seat, whose [name...]

### P R A Y E R.

Therefore, O Lord, Holy Father, Almighty Eternal God, expel this devil from this your creature [Name] <note>The letter "N." stands for *nomen* (name); the priest would insert the name of the person being exorcised here.</note> from the head, from the hair, from the crown of the head, from the forehead, from the eyes, from the tongue, from under the tongue, from the ears, from the nostrils, from the neck, from the jaws, from the teeth, from the throat, from the gums, from the mouth, from the palate, from the brain, from the membranes of the brain, from the eyelids, from the eyebrows, from the body hair, from the feet, from the shins, from the knees, from the legs, from the private parts, from the kidneys, from the sides, from the upper and lower intestines, from the thigh, from the belly, from the stomach, from the heart, from the shoulder blades, from the shoulders, from the chest, from the breasts, from the arms, from the hands, from the nails, from the bones, from the nerves, from the veins, from the marrow, from the lungs, from the structure of their limbs, from all joints, and from their whole body inside and out, and from the five senses of the body and the soul. And let him have no place in them, inside or outside, any longer, so that they may be made healthy and safe, through the invocation of the most holy name of your only-begotten Son, and through the invocation of the co-eternal Holy Spirit. You, therefore, O Almighty God, who deigned to create the body and soul of this your creature [Name], may you also deign to deliver their whole body and soul from unclean spirits and make them safe. Through our Lord Jesus Christ your Son, who lives and reigns with you in the unity of the Holy Spirit, God, forever and ever. Amen.

->*The sixth Exorcism ends.*<-

<meta>catchword: EXOR-</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the Sixth Exorcism with an exhaustive anatomical prayer, intended to drive the demon out of every specific part of the human body, from the brain to the marrow. It reflects the Renaissance belief that demonic possession was a physical occupation of the body that required a systematic "cleansing" of the anatomy.</summary>

### ¶ The following is from the Holy Gospel according to Luke.

<margin>Chapter 1.</margin>
At that time: The angel Gabriel was sent from God to a city of Galilee named Nazareth, to a virgin betrothed to a man whose name was Joseph, of the house of David; and the virgin’s name was Mary. And having come in to her, the Angel said: "Hail, full of grace, the Lord is with you: blessed are you among women." <note>original: "Ave gratia plena Dominus tecum : benedicta tu in mulieribus."</note> When she had heard this, she was troubled by his word and wondered what kind of greeting this might be. And the Angel said to her: "Do not be afraid, Mary, for you have found favor with God. Behold, you shall conceive in your womb and bear a son, and you shall call his name Jesus. He shall be great, and shall be called the Son of the Most High. And the Lord God will give him the throne of David his father, and he shall reign in the house of Jacob forever, and of his kingdom there shall be no end." But Mary said to the Angel: "How shall this happen, since I do not know a man?" And the Angel, answering, said to her: "The Holy Spirit shall come upon you, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow you. And therefore the Holy One who shall be born of you shall be called the Son of God. And behold, your kinswoman Elizabeth has also conceived a son in her old age. And this is the sixth month for her who is called barren: because no word shall be impossible with God." And Mary said: "Behold the handmaid of the Lord: <note>original: "Ecce ancilla Domini"</note> let it be done to me according to your word." ℟. Praise to you, O Christ.
