### A GREAT AND TERRIBLE REPROACH AGAINST SATAN,
#### excerpted from Prierio. <note>Silvestro Mazzolini da Prierio (c. 1456–1527) was an influential Dominican theologian and demonologist whose writings were often used in exorcism manuals.</note>

O most wretched and most iniquitous spirit; behold, I have invoked against you your Maker and your punisher; whose patience you have not been accustomed to rashly despise with impunity. You ought to have remembered how justly He punished you immediately from the beginning of your creation. Hear, most wretched one, your reproach. Almighty God <term>Adonai</term> <note>original: "Adonay." A Hebrew name for God meaning "Lord."</note>, great and wonderful, indeed incomprehensible, since He was infinite goodness and love, needing nothing but most happy in Himself; nevertheless, out of His infinite goodness, He willed to produce the universe for His own glory, but for the benefit of others; indeed, He ordained the very manifestation of His glory for the good of His creatures: for He is infinite and most pure goodness. In this universe He willed there to be rational creatures with whom He might have the friendship of holy love, and to whom He might at last grant a share in His own happiness.

<margin>Ezekiel 31.</margin>
Among these, however, your <term>Acherontine</term> <note>original: "Acherontinus." Derived from Acheron, one of the rivers of the underworld in Greek mythology, used here to mean "hellish" or "infernal."</note> master Lucifer is believed to have stood supreme. You spirits, however—now indeed brutish, but once glorious—were all brought forth by Almighty God, sublime in natural gifts, but even more sublime in those granted by grace. Yet soon, at the very beginning of your creation, you stood before your Creator not only as ungrateful but even as rebels, breaking out into pride, into envy, and the greatest hatred. O greatest ingratitude! O unheard-of audacity! O crime to be burned away by eternal flames! Hear what your great and wonderful Creator says through Isaiah concerning your prince and each of you, under the guise and figure of the most proud <term>Nebuchadnezzar</term> <note>original: "Nabuchdonosor." A biblical king of Babylon often used as a symbolic figure for Satan due to his pride.</note>; or rather, what He says of Nebuchadnezzar in the likeness of Satan.

<margin>Isaiah 14.</margin>

<meta>The catchword "Quo-" refers to the beginning of the Latin quote from Isaiah 14: "Quomodo cecidisti de caelo, Lucifer..." (How art thou fallen from heaven, Lucifer...).</meta>

<summary>This page contains a formal "Reproach" (Exprobratio) against Satan, written by the theologian Prierio. It shifts the exorcism from command to a rhetorical shaming of the demon, contrasting God's creative goodness with the fallen angels' pride and ingratitude.</summary>
<keywords>Satan, Lucifer, Prierio, pride, fall of angels, Nebuchadnezzar, Adonai, Acherontine, Isaiah 14, Ezekiel 31</keywords>

--- Page 157 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Great Reproach" against the demonic spirit, transitioning from the general description of God's goodness on the previous page to a specific, dramatic recitation of the fall of Lucifer based on the Book of Isaiah. The exorcist uses these scriptural verses to shame the demon by reminding it of its lost glory and current degradation.</meta>

<page-num>441</page-num>
<header>EXORCISM VII.</header>

"How (he says) have you fallen from heaven, O Lucifer?" <note>original: "Quomodo cecidisti de coelo Lucifer?" A citation from Isaiah 14:12, traditionally interpreted by theologians as the fall of Satan.</note> That is to say, how could there be in you such great ingratitude, such great pride, and such great iniquity, that you should suddenly fall from heaven, cast down by almighty power?

<margin>In the same place</margin>
"You who rose in the morning," <note>original: "Qui mane oriebaris."</note> meaning you who, at the very beginning of your creation, like the morning star and the most beautiful of lights, began to shine in the world. "You have fallen to the earth," namely the hellish and sulfurous earth, "you who didst wound the nations," meaning you who, like a tyrant wounding peoples, sought after dominion and exercised proud tyranny over the other spirits.

<margin>In the same place</margin>
You have fallen, I say—you who, delighted by your own beauty, said in your heart (blaspheming your Creator, whom you ought to have praised):

<margin>As above</margin>
"I will ascend into heaven," <note>original: "In coelum conscendam."</note> meaning, "I shall obtain supernatural happiness by my own power." "Above the stars of heaven," that is, above all the celestial spirits, "I will exalt my throne," wishing to be the source of grace and happiness for the rest of the angelic spirits. "I will sit on the mountain of the covenant," that is, in the heavenly Jerusalem; "on the sides of the North," <note>original: "in lateribus Aquilonis." The "North" was often associated in medieval thought with the direction of evil or the dwelling of the prideful, but here it refers to the location of the Temple in Jerusalem.</note> meaning in the midst of the Temple or the heavenly Church; for in the physical Jerusalem, the Temple was situated on the northern sides of Mount Zion.

<margin>In the same place</margin>
"I will ascend above the height of the clouds," meaning I shall transcend all created spirits in dignity and power. "I will be like the Most High God," namely the Almighty, who is blessed by His own power and not by another's, and who makes all holy spirits blessed. O word of utter recklessness! To which it is most justly replied through the great and wonderful <term>Adonai</term> <note>original: "Adonay." A Hebrew name for God meaning "The Lord."</note>: "Nevertheless, O ungrateful and most proud spirit, you shall be dragged down to hell, into the depths of the pit," because nothing is more just than to humble the proud.

<margin>In the same place</margin>
Those who see you now—after such great dignity—besieging human bodies and hiding within foul intestines, performing bestial gestures and pig-like roars mixed with feminine wailings, shall bend down in wonder to gaze at you. They will see to what depths you have descended, falling headlong after being expelled from such sublimity, and they shall say:

<margin>As above</margin>
"Is this the man?" <note>original: "Nunquid iste est vir." A quote from Isaiah 14:16.</note>—referring to "man" because of your natural intellect—but in truth, you are a beast...

<sig>Ee 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: pro-</meta>

<summary>This page contains a rhetorical shaming of the demon, using the narrative of Lucifer's fall from Isaiah 14 to contrast his original angelic beauty with his current state. The text describes the demon's prideful desire to be like God and mocks its current condition of "hiding in intestines" and making animalistic noises during possession.</summary>

<header>THE SCOURGE OF DEMONS</header>

...on account of your treacherous will, you infernal predator, who has disturbed the earth: because, having lost charity and the hope of salvation, you took care to draw the human race into your rebellion and conspiracy through your deceit. From this, infinite evils and all manner of disturbances have arisen, so much so that you established the whole world as a wasteland of divine knowledge, while this mangy beast <note>original: "bestia scabiosa." A common insulting epithet for the devil in medieval and Renaissance exorcism manuals.</note> wished to be worshipped everywhere in place of its Creator.

<margin>Chapter 28.</margin>
Indeed, most wretched one, how can you now endure that which Almighty God declares through Ezekiel concerning the most proud King of Tyre <note>Ezekiel 28 contains a prophecy against the King of Tyre, which Christian theologians traditionally interpreted as an allegory for the fall of Lucifer.</note>—addressing your own person, since you taught him by your own deed—when He says:

<margin>In the same place.</margin>
> **And you, O Cherub**—formerly a Cherub, that is, but now a predator, a beast, a serpent, and a thin, starving, and most filthy sow, so foul that the magnitude of your degradation always surpasses any speech; You were the exact seal, and a most clear one, of likeness—meaning most like unto Almighty God—full of natural wisdom, both innate and bestowed by grace, but now fuller of stupidity and most full of moral iniquity, once perfect in the beauty of grace: 
<margin>As above.</margin>
you were in the delights of God's paradise: because you were blessed in habitual hope and in the pledge of holy charity. Every precious stone—that is, every angelic spirit—was your covering: because you were adorned by the nine ranks of angels. Sardius, Topaz, and Jasper: behold one Hierarchy of a threefold order <note>The author is referencing the "Celestial Hierarchy" of Pseudo-Dionysius, which groups the nine orders of angels into three triads.</note>; Chrysolite, Onyx, and Beryl: behold the second; Sapphire, Carbuncle <note>original: "Carbunculus." A term used for deep red gems, typically garnets, believed to glow like coals.</note>, and Emerald: behold the third. Also the gold of your beauty—that is, your shining and most beautiful nature—and your settings—that is, your natural powers, namely intellect and will, in which, as if in the settings of a golden ring, the gems of spiritual habits and gifts were enclosed—these were prepared on the day you were created: because there was no imperfection in them, no lack of readiness, and no blemish. 
<margin>In the same place.</margin>
You, O Cherub—represented, that is, by the Cherub of the Holy of Holies—outstretched with a double wing, namely of contemplation and love, and protecting—that is, covering the Ark, namely of the Lord of Hosts <note>original: "domini Sabaoth." A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord of Armies" or "Lord of Hosts."</note>—that is, the heavenly Church or the corporeal creation: I placed you on the holy mountain of God, that is, in a sublime...

<header>EXORCISM VII</header>

<margin>As cited above <note>original: "Ubi supra." Referring to the prophecy in Ezekiel 28:14-16.</note></margin> You were in the celestial temple, in the midst of the burning stones—that is, the holy Angels, who burn with divine love. You walked but a single step in your first movement of being, perfect in your ways—that is, in your actions. For even if your action was but a single measure, it was nonetheless manifold in its power: namely, in knowledge and in love. This was from the day of your creation—that is, from the first instant of your existence—until iniquity was found in you in the second instant of your <margin>In the same place</margin> existence. In the multitude—that is, on account of the abundance—of your commerce—meaning your natural and bestowed gifts—your inner parts were filled with iniquity, and you sinned. You sinned irreparably because of the God-like intellect I gave you and my inviolable justice which I established. And I cast you out, proud, puffed up, and beastly, from the holy mountain of God—that is, from the Empyrean heaven <note>The Empyrean was considered the highest part of heaven in medieval and Renaissance thought, the dwelling place of God and the angels.</note>—to the infernal seats. I have destroyed you, O Cherub, you who once protected from the midst of the burning stones, a company of which you have made yourself unworthy. <margin>In the same place.</margin> You have lost your wisdom (as God is witness), though you might boast of your wisdom among foolish women <note>original: "mulierculas." A diminutive, often dismissive term for women perceived as easily deceived by the occult.</note> and necromancers <note>term: <term>Necromanticos</term>—practitioners of magic who claim to communicate with the dead or summon spirits.</note>. From an Angel, you have been made into a most foul and mangy beast.

O most wretched one, than whom no creature is more miserable or more impudent: how do you dare to compare yourself after such glory? How do you dare to orchestrate such brutal—indeed, pig-like—roars? Blush, you poor wretch, and flee, fearing the commands of Almighty God. For I, an unworthy Priest and servant of the crucified Jesus Christ of Nazareth, by the authority and power <margin>Mark 16 <note>Referring to the Gospel of Mark 16:17: "And these signs will follow those who believe: In My name they will cast out demons."</note></margin> of God the Almighty Father, † and the Son, † and the Holy Spirit, † command you to cease occupying this temple of God <note>The "temple of God" here refers to the body of the possessed person.</note>. And do not say on the Day of Judgment: "No one adjured me; no one spoke against me." Behold, you unhappy and worthless thing: I, though an unworthy Priest of Almighty God and servant of the crucified Jesus Christ of Nazareth, on behalf of Almighty God and our Lord Jesus Christ, I speak against you and command your flight. Against you I invoke as witnesses and punishers both heaven and earth; against you I invoke the Holy Trinity, the avenger of your defiance; the Blessed Virgin Mary; all the choirs of good spirits; and all the elect of God.

I humbly pray to all of them, that they may be my aid against you. As for all the hellish <note>original: "Tartareas," referring to Tartarus, the classical underworld often used in Latin texts as a synonym for Hell.</note> legions, I do not indeed invoke them (far be it from me, for by the favor of divine grace, I believe myself stronger than all the infernal powers, whether taken individually or all together). Flee, you most wretched one, flee, you thief; for the Judge is present in His anger; the deadly and blood-red torturer is here, who hastens to hang you—like a thief and a robber—upon the fork of the infernal gallows <note>term: <term>patibuli</term>—a gallows or cross-shaped frame used for execution; here used metaphorically for the demon's spiritual punishment.</note>. Through the prayers and conjurations of this book, and through all their powers, and through all the signs and words of our Savior Jesus Christ, and through all the prayers that are in heaven and on earth, and through all the powers of the heavens in their strength, and through the power of the Holy Names of God, I command you that hereafter you must not, nor can you, remain here, but you shall do whatever we have commanded you. Otherwise, we shall cast you into the lake of fire and sulfur, to be eternally burned. Therefore, in whatever part of this body you may be, whether inside or out, come and see the sign of the Holy Cross † consecrated by the supreme Creator, our Lord Jesus Christ; for by His power you are bound to obey us. And by Him we bind you, so that you must come before us even against your will; because the words of God which come out of our mouth are most fervent fires which will burn you eternally. This is the sign through which the whole world trembles, and every creature quakes—and yet you are rebels against our Creator and yours? Therefore, be cursed, and excommunicated, and blasphemed in eternal punishment; and let there be no rest for you at any hour,

*Since it often happens that the <term>Energumens</term><note>A term derived from Greek used to describe individuals "worked upon" or possessed by an unclean spirit.</note> cannot be delivered from the unclean spirits harassing them on the first, second, or third attempt, therefore the Priest, wishing to bring the conjuration to a conclusion, should command the spirits that have departed from the body in this manner.*

### THE COMMAND TO BE GIVEN TO ALL
### unclean spirits having departed from possessed bodies.

I command all you unclean spirits who have departed from this body, in the name of the most holy Trinity, of the Fa†ther, and of the † Son, and of the Holy † Spirit, that you have no authority, power, or strength to show yourselves to this creature of God [Name], nor to create any illusion for them, whether imaginary <note>original: "phantasticam," referring to mental hallucinations or visions.</note> or real, nor may you offend or harm them in body or in soul; nor may you return again to harass them, nor send other demons in your place. But being bound by the aforementioned command, go to your infernal seats, or to the places assigned to you by God, where you can harm no creature, whether rational <note>Human beings.</note> or irrational <note>Animals.</note>. <margin>Revelation, chapter 5.</margin> Behold the Cross † of the Lord, flee you hostile powers; the Lion of the tribe of Judah, the root of David, has conquered. <note>This formula, known as the Brief of St. Anthony, uses messianic titles from the Book of Revelation to assert Christ's victory over evil.</note>

*If the <term>Energumen</term><note>A term for a person possessed by an unclean spirit, from the Greek word meaning "one being worked upon."</note> has not been freed, and yet (due to urgent necessity) the conjuration must be ended for a time, then command all the spirits remaining in the body—forcing them—to withdraw from the head, from the heart, and from the stomach, and to descend to the lower parts of the body, specifically into the dead nails of the feet. # THANKSGIVINGS
## For the liberated <term>Energumen</term><note>A term for a person who was possessed by a demon, derived from the Greek word for "one being worked upon" or "driven."</note>.

->*When the possessed person has been freed, say:*<-

<margin>Saint Ambrose and Saint Augustine<note>Tradition holds that this hymn, the *Te Deum*, was composed spontaneously by these two saints on the occasion of Augustine's baptism in 387 AD.</note></margin>
We praise you, O God: we acknowledge you to be the Lord.
All the earth venerates you: the Father everlasting.
To you all Angels: to you the heavens and all the powers therein.
To you the Cherubim and Seraphim: cry out with unceasing voice:
Holy, Holy, Holy: Lord God of <term>Sabaoth</term><note>A Hebrew word meaning "Hosts" or "Armies," referring to God's command over the celestial forces.</note>.
Heaven and earth are full: of the majesty of your glory.
The glorious choir: of the Apostles praises you.
The admirable number: of the Prophets praises you.
The white-robed army of Martyrs: praises you.
The holy Church throughout the whole world: acknowledges you.
The Father: of infinite majesty.
Your adorable, true: and only Son.
Also the Holy Spirit: the Comforter.
You, O Christ: are the King of glory.
You are the everlasting: Son of the Father.
When you took it upon yourself to deliver man: you did not abhor the Virgin’s womb.
When you had overcome the sting of death: you opened the kingdom of heaven to believers.
You sit at the right hand of God: in the glory of the Father.
We believe that you: will come to be our Judge.
We therefore pray you, help your servants: whom you have redeemed with your precious blood.
Make them to be numbered with your Saints: in everlasting glory.
O Lord, save your people: and bless your inheritance.
And govern them: and lift them up forever.
Day by day: we bless you.
And we praise your name forever: even world without end.
Vouchsafe, O Lord, this day: to keep us without sin.
Have mercy on us, O Lord: have mercy on us.

### PRAYER

**W**e give thanks to you, holy Lord, almighty Father, eternal God: we give thanks to you, O Christ, Word of the eternal Father: we give thanks to you, nourishing Spirit: we give thanks to all, both Angels and men: because by the almighty power of God the dragon <note>original: "draco"; a common scriptural and liturgical metaphor for the Devil or a high-ranking demon</note> has been cast down through our hands. Who lives and reigns forever and ever. Amen.

### PRAYER

<margin>Isaiah 11.</margin>
**A**lmighty eternal God, who has deigned to liberate this your servant [Name] from every harassment of Satan and his ministers: send into him your sevenfold Holy Spirit, the Comforter <note>original: "paraclytum", from the Greek *parakletos*, meaning an advocate or helper</note>, from the heavens. R. Amen. The Spirit of wisdom and understanding. R. Amen. The Spirit of counsel and fortitude. R. Amen. The Spirit of knowledge and piety. R. Amen. Fill him with the Spirit of your fear <note>The "fear of the Lord" is traditionally the seventh gift, representing awe and reverence for the divine</note>, and mercifully mark him for eternal life. R. Amen.
V. The Lord be with you.
R. And with your spirit.

### EXORCISM OF SALT.

<margin>The Catholic Church</margin>
<margin>4 Kings 2 <note>Now known in modern Bibles as 2 Kings 2:19-22, where the prophet Elisha purifies a spring of water by casting salt into it.</note></margin>
I exorcise you, creature of salt <note>original: "creatura salis"; in this context, "creature" refers to a created element of the natural world being repurposed for a sacred task.</note>, by the living † God, by the true † God, by the holy † God, by the God who commanded you to be cast into the water by Elisha the Prophet so that the barrenness of the water might be healed: that you may become exorcised salt for the salvation of believers, and be health of soul and body to all who take you; and may every phantasm <note>A deceptive or haunting vision produced by a demon.</note> and wickedness, or trickery of diabolical fraud, and every adjured <note>commanded under oath</note> unclean spirit, flee and depart from the place in which you have been sprinkled. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

<header>MOST EFFECTIVE REMEDIES</header>

## PRAYER.

<margin>From the same source <note>original: "ibidem"; referring to the Roman Ritual or the liturgical source cited on the previous page.</note></margin>
We humbly implore Your boundless mercy, almighty eternal God, that You may deign in Your piety to bless † and sancti † fy this creature of salt <note>original: "creaturam salis"; a "creature" here refers to a created element of the natural world.</note> which You have provided for the use of the human race; let it bring health of mind and body to all who take it. May whatever is touched or sprinkled by it be free from all uncleanness and from every assault of spiritual wickedness. Through our Lord Jesus Christ Your Son, etc.

## EXORCISM OF THE WATER.

<margin>From the same source.</margin>
I exorcise you, creature of water, in the name of God the Father † Almighty, and in the name of † Jesus Christ His Son our Lord, and in the power of the Holy † Spirit; that you may become exorcised water to put to flight every power of the enemy, and that you may be able to uproot and cast out the enemy himself with his apostate angels. By the power of the same our Lord Jesus Christ, who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

## PRAYER.

O God, who for the salvation of the human race has established the greatest sacraments <note>The Latin "sacramenta" here refers to the sacred signs and mysteries of the faith, particularly baptism, which uses water.</note> in the substance of water; mercifully hear our prayers, and pour the power of Your bless † ing into this element, prepared by manifold purifications. May this, Your creature, serving in Your mysteries, receive the effect of divine grace to drive away demons and dispel diseases. May whatever this water sprinkles in the homes or places of the faithful be free from all uncleanness and delivered from harm. Let no pestilential spirit linger there, nor any corrupting air; may all the snares of the lurking enemy depart. If there be anything that envies the safety or the peace of those who dwell there, may it flee at the sprinkling of this water, so that the health sought through the invocation of Your holy name may be defended against all assaults. Through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc.

## PRAYER.

O God, author of invincible strength and King of an insuperable empire, always the magnificent conqueror, who restrains the forces of the adversary's dominion, who overcomes the cruelty of the roaring enemy, and who powerfully defeats hostile wickedness: O Lord, we, trembling and suppliant, beseech and ask You that You may graciously look upon this creature <note>The Latin "creaturam" refers to a created element of nature, here salt and water.</note> of salt and water, kindly enlighten it, and sanctify it with the dew of Your piety. Grant that wherever it is sprinkled, through the invocation of Your holy name, every infestation of the unclean spirit may be driven away, and the terror of the poisonous serpent be cast far out. May the presence of the Holy Spirit be deigned to be with us everywhere as we ask for Your mercy. Through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc.

I exorcise you, most unclean spirit, every attack of Satan, and every phantom, in the name of God the Fa†ther, in the name of God the So†n, and in the name of the Holy † Spirit, that you depart from this oil, so that it may become a spiritual potion <note>original: "potatio"; refers to the practice of consuming blessed oil as a medicinal and spiritual remedy.</note> for healing the sick; so that the Holy Spirit may dwell within them, through the name of God the Father, through the name of our Lord Jesus Christ, His most beloved Son. ### PRAYER.

<margin>Numbers 16 <note>A reference to the biblical account of incense and atonement stopping a plague.</note></margin>
Send forth, O Lord, the sevenfold <note>The "sevenfold" Spirit refers to the gifts of the Holy Spirit: wisdom, understanding, counsel, fortitude, knowledge, piety, and fear of the Lord.</note> Holy Spirit, the Comforter from heaven, into this richness of the olive, which You have deigned to produce from a green tree for the restoration of mind and body. May it be, by Your blessing, a constant protection of mind and body for everyone who touches it, for the clearing away of all melancholic humors <term>humores melancholicos: in early medicine, "black bile" was thought to cause not just sadness, but spiritual vulnerability to demons</term>, all pains, all infirmities, and all sicknesses of mind and body; with this You have anointed Kings, Prophets, and Martyrs with Your perfect holy unction. Show us Your mercy, O Lord, so that it may remain blessed within the vitals of Your servants, in the name of the Lord Jesus Christ. Who lives and reigns with You and the Holy Spirit through all ages of ages. Amen.

I adjure you, O Satan, and all evil spirits, by the name of the living † God, the true † God, the holy † God; by the name and the merits of the Virgin Mary; by the power of all heavenly spirits; and by the nine heavenly orders <note>The "nine orders" refers to the traditional hierarchy of angels: Seraphim, Cherubim, Thrones, Dominions, Virtues, Powers, Principalities, Archangels, and Angels.</note>, into a curse, confusion, and a hundredfold increase of your punishments from day to day, to be increased until the last, terrible day of the final Judgment, if you do not now depart, accursed Satan, from this servant of God, [Name] <note>The letter "N." in liturgical texts stands for the Latin "Nomen" (Name), where the practitioner speaks the name of the person receiving the rite.</note>. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

## CONJURATION.

<margin>Exodus 16 <note>While the text later references the rebellion in Numbers 16, this margin note likely refers to the bread from heaven or a specific liturgical parallel found in older editions of the Vulgate or lectionaries.</note></margin>
Again I conjure you, O Satan, so that <gloss>quod</gloss> you may not rest, nor <gloss>neque</gloss> cease from continuous labor until you are removed entirely from the place where you are, and from the damned company of whatever princedom you belong to; and [I conjure you] to remove your own and every evil spell cast upon this creature of God, [Name], as well as the infirmities and pains of soul and body, by the words of the aforesaid conjuration. Accursed Satan, may your name and your work be confounded and cursed for eternity and beyond by Almighty God, and by me, through the power handed to me over you—not only through baptism and the other sacraments, but also through the administration of the true body and blood of our Lord Jesus Christ, granted to me by the authority of the blessed Apostles Peter and Paul. And if you are rebellious, defiant, and disobedient to me, I conjure against you and against all you hellish spirits: [I command] you wicked spirits with haste, insofar as by the power of that name which Moses spoke; and then Dathan and Abiram <note>Dathan and Abiram were biblical figures who rebelled against the leadership of Moses; according to the Book of Numbers, the earth opened and swallowed them alive as punishment.</note> were swallowed by the caverns of the Abyss; and by the power of that name, may they bind you back into the depths of the abyss until the final day. Amen.

# INSTRUCTION FOR
## EXORCISTS.

*The Exorcist must take care that he does not, under any circumstances, provide this oil or any other drinkable remedy <term>drinkable remedy: "remedium potabile," a liquid preparation intended to be consumed as part of a spiritual or physical cure</term> to any sick person without the advice and judgment of Physicians. This is because the complexions <note>original: "complexiones." In early modern medicine, this refers to a person's "humoral temperament" or physical constitution—the specific balance of blood, phlegm, black bile, and yellow bile.</note> or natures of the sick are diverse, and therefore these and other remedies must be applied to them in different ways. For what will sometimes be beneficial to one, to another it could—*

### AGAINST MALIGNANT SPIRITS

...of the Jews? For we have seen his star in the East, and have come to worship him. When King Herod heard this, he was troubled, and all Jerusalem with him. And assembling all the chief priests and scribes of the people, he inquired of them where the Christ was to be born. And they said to him, "In Bethlehem of Judea; for so it is written by the Prophet: *And you, Bethlehem, in the land of Judah, are by no means least among the rulers of Judah; for from you shall come a leader who will shepherd my people Israel.*" Then Herod, secretly calling the Magi <term>Magi: wise men or astrologers from the East, traditionally viewed as the first Gentiles to recognize Christ</term>, diligently learned from them the time the star appeared to them. And sending them to Bethlehem, he said, "Go and search diligently for the child, and when you have found him, report back to me, so that I also may come and worship him." 

When they had heard the king, they departed. And behold, the star which they had seen in the East went before them, until it came and stood over where the child was. When they saw the star, they rejoiced with exceeding great joy. And entering the house, they found the child with Mary his mother. And falling down, they worshiped him. And opening their treasures, they offered him gifts: gold, frankincense, and myrrh. And having received an answer in a dream that they should not return to Herod, they went back to their own country by another way.

<margin>
The condemnation of the Council of Braga.
</margin>
6. ...of its decrees, stating: "If anyone says that human souls previously sinned in a heavenly dwelling, and for this reason were cast down into human bodies on earth, as Priscillian <note>original: "Priscillianus"; referring to the 4th-century Spanish bishop whose gnostic-leaning views on the pre-existence of the soul were condemned as heresy.</note> said, let him be <term>anathema</term> <note>original: "anathema sit"; a formal curse or excommunication by the Church.</note>."

## CHAPTER V.

### *Whether it is permitted in any way to resort to sorcerers to regain health, and how spells or instruments of witchcraft can be destroyed.*

<margin>
Certain possessed persons cannot be freed.
</margin>
Because it often happens that Exorcists have in their hands some <term>energumenos</term> <note>From the Greek 'energoumenos', meaning those 'worked upon' or driven by a spirit; a standard term for the possessed.</note> and bewitched persons whom (by the commanding or permitting will of God) they cannot free; and one might ask what should be done in such a case when health cannot be obtained through Exorcisms. Therefore, lest blind Exorcists fall into error, they should take notice of those impediments placed in our *Flagellum* <note>The *Flagellum Daemonum* (Whip of Demons), a famous exorcism manual by Girolamo Menghi.</note>, chapter 17, and remove them, both on their own part and on the part of the patient. And if, after all these have been removed, health still cannot be obtained, they should apply all the remedies which we have placed in the third book of our *Compendium* <note>The *Compendium Artis Exorcisticae* (Compendium of the Exorcistic Art), another work by Menghi.</note>.

<margin>
Whether it is lawful to resort to sorcerers.
</margin>
6. However, after the application of all these things, is it permitted to resort to sorcerers <note>original: "sortilegos"; literally 'lot-casters', used here to mean folk healers, "cunning folk," or white witches who used magic to counter magic.</note> to obtain health? Different people have said different things. Some have said that it is, provided the sick person does not induce the sorcerer to perform any evil deed; because (as they say) it is permitted for me to use the evil of another for my own good. They give the example of the usurer <note>original: "usurario"; a moneylender charging interest, which was considered a sin in medieval/early modern theology.</note>, who is always ready to lend money at interest. This opinion, even if it is supported by certain reasons that seem to have some appearance of truth, is nevertheless attacked by many and seen as a cause or occasion for error; therefore, it must be avoided as dangerous, lest it lead some to ruin.

<margin>
The faithful must beware of this opinion.
</margin>
Others say, without any distinction, that we can in no way have recourse to sorcerers to obtain health, and that we ought rather to endure all evils, even death, than to use their assistance, since evil things—that is, sins—are in no way to be done so that good may come of them. This opinion, though it is difficult, yet...

<margin>
A difficult and very firm opinion.
</margin>

<meta>catchword: is (est)</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the theological condemnation of the pre-existence of souls and addresses whether it is morally permissible for a possessed person to seek help from sorcerers or folk healers when traditional exorcisms fail.</summary>
<keywords>Council of Braga, Priscillian, sorcery, exorcism, usury, moral theology, healing, Girolamo Menghi</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from Chapter V regarding the moral and practical dilemmas faced when exorcisms fail. # BEAUTIFUL DOCTRINE

<margin>
The opinion of Scotus regarding recourse to sorcerers.
</margin>
...is more secure, because it places its followers on the safer side—namely, so that they are not exposed to the danger of mortal sin and their own damnation. There is another opinion, which seems to belong to the Scotists <note>Followers of John Duns Scotus (c. 1266–1308), a hugely influential Franciscan theologian and philosopher known as the "Subtle Doctor."</note>, which holds a middle ground between the aforementioned opinions and says that it is permitted in some way to have recourse to sorcerers or <term>malefici</term> <note>original: "maleficos"; practitioners of harmful magic or witchcraft.</note> for the sake of recovering health. For a fuller declaration of this, it must be known that sorcerers, in the crafting of their spells, mostly use some external "witchcraft wood," which is always a vile thing of no value; this is called a "witchcraft instrument" because this wood is involved in the crafting of the spells. For the sorcerers have demons ready at their beck and call for evil; and as often as they wish to craft some spell, they call upon them, and having pacts and agreements, they assign such

<margin>
The pacts of sorcerers with demons.
</margin>
instruments to them with this agreement: that as long as such wood shall last, for so long shall such and such a person be tortured in such and such a place; and that by the removal of the wood, or the witchcraft instrument, the injury caused by that demon is also removed. Sorcerers are also accustomed to hide such wood so that it cannot easily be found and removed by anyone.

<margin>
To destroy witchcraft is lawful.
</margin>
<margin>
Other instruments.
</margin>
Regarding our proposal, who doubts that a sorcerer, without a new pact with a demon, can destroy that wood and remove the injury inflicted by the demon according to the original pact? I say, therefore, that to have recourse to them in this way—namely, so that they destroy that wood without a new pact—is not illicit. And this is what Scotus says in his work on the *Sentences* <note>original: "in 4. dist. 34. q. unica"; referring to Scotus's commentary on the Fourth Book of Peter Lombard's Sentences, Distinction 34, the only question.</note>, and these are his formal words: "The

<margin>
The most resolute Scotist doctrine.
</margin>
second type of impotence is caused by malignant spirits, who, having pacts with men, keep their promises—not because they are truthful, but so that others might cling to them; for if they kept no pacts, no one would serve them, and so that those to whom they keep promises might worship them. For out of their pride, they especially hunger for divine honors.

<margin>
Why demons hunger for honors.
</margin>
God also permits them to exercise their power to hinder some people from certain acts which they would be capable of if they were not hindered; and this is either because of their sins, or because of some other hidden cause. And in this way a <term>Maga</term> <note>A female sorceress or witch.</note> having a pact with a demon procures from him that he might hinder a man from such an act with such a woman, as long as such a spell persists—for example, a curved needle...

<summary>This page explores the theological "middle way" of the Scotists, arguing that victims may seek the help of a sorcerer to destroy the physical "instrument" of a spell (like a piece of wood) without committing a sin, provided no new pact is made with a demon.</summary>

<meta>The text continues a discussion on the nature of divine action, following a previous page that likely defined miracles "above nature." The catchword "respon-" indicates the next page begins with "respondeo" or a similar word of reply.</meta>

# BEAUTIFUL DOCTRINE
## 162

[God] will lead in the bodies of the elect; and as is the **Incarnation of the Word**. Or because, even if [nature] could induce such a form into some matter, it cannot do so in this or that specific case; just as nature has the power to cause life in general, but nature cannot cause life to return to *this* particular dead man.

<margin>
What a miracle **against nature** is like.
</margin>
Something is said to be **against nature** when there remains in nature a disposition contrary to the effect that God is producing. This occurred when He preserved the young men unharmed in the furnace <note>A reference to the story of Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego from the Book of Daniel</note>, while the power of burning still remained in the fire; and when the water of the Jordan River stood still, while its weight <note>original: "gravitate"; the natural tendency of water to flow downward</note> remained within it; and it is a similar case that a virgin gave birth.

<margin>
What miracles **beyond nature** are, and how they occur.
</margin>
However, God is said to act **beyond nature** when He produces an effect that nature *is* capable of producing, but in a way that nature cannot. This happens either because the instruments by which nature works are lacking—as when Christ turned water into wine (which nature can indeed do in a certain way when water is taken up as nourishment for a vine and, in its own time, is produced as the juice of the grape after being fully digested)—or because God (who is blessed) has more ways of operating than nature does. This also applies to the element of time: for instance, when someone is healed immediately at the invocation of a saint, whereas nature could not heal them immediately, but only gradually and over a different span of time.

<margin>
Demons can perform **marvels**, but not miracles.
</margin>
Demons, therefore, according to <term>Saint Bonaventure</term> <note>A 13th-century scholastic theologian and philosopher</note> (in Book 2, distinction 7, article 2, question 3), can perform things that appear to be, and truly are, **marvels**, but they are not **miracles**. This is because miracles cannot be performed by the force of nature, but only by a power *above* nature, which belongs to the First Agent <note>God</note>. Miracles are above natural faculty and therefore testify to the omnipotence of the Maker. Thus, the works performed by demons—even if they seem like miracles because they happen in an almost imperceptible amount of time—are not true miracles because they are performed by the force of nature. And if you object that nature cannot work so quickly, I say that while this is true of nature acting by itself, it can be so assisted and strengthened by the application of suitable natural things that it breaks forth into an effect almost suddenly. This is evident in the <term>spontaneous generation of frogs</term> <note>A common medieval belief that certain animals could be born from inanimate matter under specific conditions</note>, which are known to be born in a very short time during periods of intense heat through <term>antiparistasis</term> <note>original: "antiparistasin"; a philosophical concept where a quality is intensified when surrounded by its opposite</note>. And if anyone should ask how such miracles can be distinguished...

<summary>This page distinguishes between miracles "against nature" (suspending natural properties) and "beyond nature" (achieving natural ends through supernatural means). It also clarifies that demons only perform "marvels" using natural forces, whereas true miracles require God's omnipotence.</summary>

<keywords>miracles, Saint Bonaventure, laws of nature, demons vs God, water into wine, spontaneous generation, antiparistasis, scholastic theology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the scholastic investigation into miracles begun on the previous page. Having defined what a miracle is in relation to nature, the author now addresses how to distinguish between genuine divine miracles and the "wonders" performed by demons or sorcerers.</meta>

# ON EVIL SPIRITS
### Nn
<meta>catchword: CHAPTER</meta>

<margin>How true miracles can be discerned, and in what ways they differ from false ones.</margin>
The answer is that even if they might appear similar in their outward form, they differ in four ways: in their primary agent, in their minister, in their purpose, and in the nature of the act itself. Regarding the **primary agent**, they differ because in divine miracles, an agent of infinite power acts above nature. In the miracles of demons, however, the power of nature is at work, assisted in many ways by other natural forces. Regarding the **minister** <note>The human or creaturely instrument through whom the miracle is channeled</note>, they also differ. For God’s miracles are performed through "public justice," according to truth and outward appearance; but the miracles of demons are performed through private contracts and certain secrets.

<margin>God's miracles are performed through public justice.</margin>
This is what the holy Augustine says: that good people perform miracles through "public justice" <note>original: "publicam iustitiam"; a theological concept referring to power derived from the universal, lawful order of God rather than personal or hidden pacts</note>, which is to say, through the true faith which Truth proclaims to all. Wicked Christians perform them through the *signs* of public justice, because they possess faith and appear to have good works; but **Mages** perform them through private contracts. This is what is described in the Book of Exodus <note>Refers to the magicians of Pharaoh in Exodus 7-8</note> as being done through incantations and certain secrets. The sign of this is that these are works of darkness, because he who acts wickedly hates the light.

<margin>True miracles are performed for the glory of God and the benefit of one's neighbor.</margin>
Similarly, they differ in their **purpose**. For God’s miracles are performed for the sake of utility and divine glory; but the miracles of Mages are performed for deception, for vain glory, and for self-exaltation; hence they are almost always useless.

<margin>Why God permits the miracles of demons.</margin>
They also differ in the **nature of the act itself**: because demons perform miracles of this sort that are trivial and useless, whereas divine miracles are profoundly useful and necessary. This is why demons primarily produce frogs and serpents <note>Commonly cited examples of "spontaneous generation" in ancient science, and a reference to the Egyptian plagues</note>, because these are easily produced through natural operations. By divine judgment, these are permitted as a reminder of past deeds; for just as God tempers the power and cunning of the devil—allowing him to perversely imitate Him so that through this very imitation the devil might seduce the wicked according to their own lack of merit—He also permits these things so that they might be detected by the good, and so that faith might have its place alongside miracles. For if only God and His servants performed miracles, who would not believe in God, being compelled by the wonders? But now, since both the wicked and the good perform miracles, those who are divinely enlightened are helped so they can discern true miracles among them; thus they are exercised and tested by the miracles of the demons.

<summary>This page explains the four criteria for distinguishing divine miracles from demonic ones—agent, minister, purpose, and result—and argues that God permits demonic wonders to test the faith of the righteous.</summary>
<keywords>miracles, demons, public justice, St. Augustine, Mages, Exodus, discernment of spirits, sorcery</keywords>

BEAUTIFUL DOCTRINE <note>original: "DOCTR. PULCHER."; likely a shortened title for the volume or section, such as "Doctrina Pulchra" or "The Fair Doctrine"</note>

# CHAPTER XI.

## *Concerning the causes and methods by which demons enter human bodies, and the signs and effects by which a person is recognized as being demon-possessed.*

<margin>Demons possess innumerable arts against the human race.</margin>
As we stated above in Chapter VIII and often elsewhere, demons possess a thousand methods and arts for harming the human race. While all people ought to know these in order to avoid sins and to repel their snares, Exorcists in particular must understand them—specifically those through which demons most offend the soul and the body. This occurs precisely when demons harass human bodies. Therefore, a skilled Exorcist must know everything pertaining to **conjuration** <term>conjuration: the formal ritual of commanding a demon to depart by the authority of a higher power, in this case, Jesus Christ</term> so that (with God’s help) he may be able to free those harassed by that atrocious and terrible affliction, acting as a minister of our Lord Jesus Christ.

<margin>There is no lack of those who disparage exorcism.</margin>
For a clearer explanation of this matter, one must know that there have always been, and still are, men who strive with all their might to deny that the possessed, sorcery, and victims of sorcery <note>original: "maleficiatos"; those suffering from "maleficia" or harmful magic</note> can be found today. Furthermore, they assert that all illnesses attributed to sorcery are natural, and that their signs are not from sorcery but are purely natural in origin. They attempt to prove this through various arguments, as was shown above in Chapter VII, which I omit here for the sake of brevity.

<margin>Signs of sorcery may be common to other things.</margin>
<margin>How signs of sorcery are distinguished from natural ones.</margin>
It should be noted, therefore, that even if some signs used to recognize someone as being demon-possessed or a victim of sorcery may be common to both natural and supernatural infirmities—and consequently may be dismissed by critics—true and real signs can nonetheless be recognized and distinguished from natural ones. This is especially true when physicians do not recognize such infirmities, do not know how to apply effective remedies to heal the sick person, and when the medicines applied to the patient provide no benefit at all.

<margin>For what causes demons enter human bodies.</margin>
It must also be understood that demons enter human bodies for various reasons, as we stated in our *Compendium* <note>A reference to another work by the author, likely a handbook for priests or exorcists</note>, Book II, Chapter 11. For sometimes a person is tormented by the devil for a greater accumulation of merits <note>Similar to the biblical story of Job, where suffering is allowed to prove the person's faith</note>; sometimes one is tormented because of the **venial sin** <term>venial sin: in Catholic theology, a minor sin that damages but does not destroy the relationship with God</term> of another; and sometimes it proceeds from one’s own venial sin; while at other times it is be-

<margin>Demons appear under various forms.</margin>
Before demons enter human bodies, they are accustomed to appearing to their victims in some terrible, deformed, or horrific shape—either as humans or as beasts. Although they seem to vanish suddenly, they nonetheless enter into their bodies. 

<margin>Demons enter human bodies in different ways.</margin>
This usually happens at night and in shadowy, dark places lacking light. They enter the possessed person through the mouth, the ears, or the nostrils, like a gust of wind or some sort of mouse <note>original: "muris"; this refers to the common folkloric and demonological belief that spirits could take the shape of small, quick animals to slip into the body</note>. Alternatively, they may send terrifying dreams to those sleeping, frightening them in their slumber; those people later wake up and are found with the signs of demon possession. 

<margin>Demons tear human bodies upon their entry.</margin>
Sometimes, as demons enter human bodies, they tear their limbs in a terrifying manner. At other times during the entry, it seems to those being tormented as if a vessel of freezing cold water is being poured down their backs, which then runs back and forth through the entire body, from head to foot.

<margin>Demons work various effects in possessed bodies.</margin>
The effects that are usually produced in tormented people by unclean spirits are almost innumerable, yet I will list some here so that Exorcists may proceed with caution regarding the **conjuration** <term>conjuration: the formal ritual command used by an exorcist to expel a demon</term>.

<margin>Saul pursues David with an obstinate mind.</margin>
he persecuted David most bitterly even unto death, despite there being no cause for it.

He was subsequently freed by the Savior himself.

### CHAPTER XII.

### Regarding spells, and the signs by which one is known to be possessed or bewitched.

<margin>A pact between the demon and the witch is present in every spell.</margin>
There is no doubt that humans can be affected by **spells** <note>original: "maleficiis"; specifically harmful magic or witchcraft</note>, as experience testifies and as we have said in our *Compendium*, Books 2 and 3. Therefore, with this foundation laid, it must be known that in all spells—whether they are made to induce love or to cause harm—there always intervenes a pact, whether manifest or secret, tacit or expressed, which is held between the witch and the demon, and it is always a vain and empty thing. Once this is completed, the demon immediately enters, either personally, as in the **Possessed** <note>original: "Energumenos"; those whose bodies are physically occupied by a spirit</note>, or through his power, as in the **Bewitched** <note>original: "maleficiatos"; those suffering from external magical influence</note>, and he enters only that person against whom the said spell was crafted.
<margin>A very useful instruction for Exorcists is described here.</margin>
Regarding what should be done with those instruments of witchcraft found in beds or other places—such as bones, feathers, hair, grains, iron, stones, nails, sulfur, needles, and similar things brought by demons—I say that all must be burned with blessed fire, as we have ordained in our *Scourge* <note>original: "Flagello"; likely a reference to the author's work *Flagellum Daemonum*</note>, leaving aside all other superstition. <margin>By what signs a bewitched person may be recognized.</margin>As to the signs by which one may be known to be both bewitched and possessed at the same time, I say that although there are many, I will for the present include those found in the *Sacerdotale* <note>A manual of liturgical rites for the use of priests</note>. Sometimes the demon passes from the head all the way to the feet like a wind, and vice versa. The demons produce many other signs in obsessed bodies, which you will see in practice; for different demons produce different signs. Finally, certain demons are found who, in
<margin>Demons sometimes speak wonders in the bodies of the possessed.</margin>
possessed bodies, speak to the one performing the rite (as we have often said) concerning the deepest mysteries of the Holy Scripture; they are thus compelled by God, and sometimes do so voluntarily, so that they may draw out and lead the minds of the Exorcists and those assisting into curiosity. To these, the Exorcist should impose silence as much as possible, and command them that, in leaving the possessed bodies, they leave them healthy, free, and unencumbered as they found them.
<margin>Let the Exorcist ask for signs of the demon's departure.</margin>
And he should compel them to provide some real and effective sign of their departure, and that they leave truly and effectively, taking with them their whole damned company. Some demons sometimes go out through the mouth in the manner of a flame of fire. Others, indeed, in the manner of a frozen wind. Some of them, however, in the form of bees or ants. Others go out through the ears, departing from the stomach, from the heart, and from all parts of the obsessed body. Some depart through the bowels <note>original: "per secessum"; literally "by means of the latrine" or "through evacuation."</note> in the likeness of a ball, which always rolls in a circle...
